# Functions attempted and parked, with the class of difference. Read by
# tools_src/candidates.py so they are not offered again. See docs/DECISIONS.md
# for what each class means and what has already been tried.
#
# When parking a function, put its best candidate C in parked/<func>.c. The
# first 37 entries below were parked without doing that, and the near-miss
# source is gone -- which means the flag sweep and the permuter can never be
# re-run on them without decompiling from scratch. That mattered the moment
# -fno-schedule-insns turned out to have been wrongly excluded from the sweep
# set: the functions most likely to be unblocked by it are exactly the ones
# whose candidates were thrown away. Only func_80040390 and func_800594C0
# survived, because they happened to still be in scratch.
#
# Best flag combination per candidate, from tools_src/sweep_try.py over the
# whole set (2026-07-26). The count is differing instructions under that
# combination; the source shape is whatever is in parked/<func>.c. Two entries
# that were on this list -- func_8001798C and func_80049394 -- matched outright
# under -mno-split-addresses and are in src/ now.
#
# THESE ARE SEARCH RESULTS, NOT MEASUREMENTS. sweep_try.py runs through
# try_func, which is validated against src/ and parked/ under the flags those
# functions actually use -- not against arbitrary combinations. Its assembler
# flags go through a per-combination flag_overrides.json write, which is
# exactly the kind of path that has produced confident wrong numbers in this
# project before. Confirm with a full build before any of these becomes a
# PER_FUNC_FLAGS entry. func_8001798C and func_80049394 were confirmed that
# way; nothing else in this table has been.
#
#   func_80016DDC     11  O1 G0
#   func_8002CCE4      3  O2 G0
#   func_8002F440     14  O1 G8
#   func_80036F80     21  O2 G8 nosched1
#   func_800382A8     33  O2 G0 nosched2
#   func_80039E9C      6  O1 G0
#   func_80040390      6  O1 G8
#   func_80040510     29  O1 G0
#   func_80045484      8  O2 G0 nosched2 macro
#   func_800472A8     10  O1 G8
#   func_8004733C     10  O2 G8
#   func_80048C0C      3  O1 G0
#   func_8004A854     13  O2 G0 nosched2 macro
#   func_8004C84C     19  O2 G8 nosched1+2
#   func_800594C0     16  O1 G0
#   func_8005F7B0      5  O2 G8
#   func_800732A0     13  O1 G8 macro
#
# func_8003201C is a clean instance: 40 instructions against 40, the whole
# sequence identical, and two symmetric pairs swapped -- (card, its copy) in
# the outer loop and (count, index) in the trailing one. Four declaration
# orders, a hoisted slot pointer and && vs nested ifs all leave it at 17; the
# sweep's best is O1 G0 at 13, which fixes the register names and loses the
# strength-reduced pointer instead, so it is not closer.
# --- register allocator: retail copies a value out of a register and we do
# not. func_80037110 escaped this by declaring a *second* pointer at the top
# and using it for only part of the function -- not `p = arg0` used
# everywhere, which does nothing. Tried on func_800382A8, func_800472A8 and
# func_80040510 afterwards and it moved none of them, so it is a shape that
# works sometimes rather than the answer. Two variables each covering part of
# the body is the thing to try first here.
# ---
# func_80070710 is ten instructions, sequence identical, every register name
# different: retail reuses $a0 as the %hi temp of the scalar load it lands in,
# and puts the table address in $v0 where we put $v1. Six source forms and both
# -G settings all sit at 8.
# ---
# func_80071424 and func_80071460 are `D_800F5B98[func_8007058C()] = <sym>[0];`
# and they are the sharpest instance of the class, because their siblings
# func_8007164C and func_80071688 -- the same store, a different source symbol
# -- match as that exact one-liner. Retail's two functions disagree about the
# registers for otherwise identical code: the siblings put the table in $a0 and
# the loaded value in $v1, these two swap them.
# Sixteen source forms brought them 6 -> 2, and the two remaining differences
# are **coupled**, which is what makes it a park rather than a puzzle:
#   - value read into a named variable (`s32 v = SYM[0]; t[i] = v;`) gets both
#     registers right, and gcc then emits `la; lb; sll`, one slot late;
#   - value read inline in the store gets the order and the addu destination
#     right, and swaps the two registers.
# No form gives both, and the sweep's best is O2 G0 at 2 for the first shape,
# 6 for the second, so no flag reaches across it either. The parked candidate
# is the first shape.
# Permuter run 2026-08-03 on both: func_80071460's best scored 40 and try_func
# calls it 10, four worse than the plain inline form; func_80071424's best
# scored 40 and try_func calls it 6, four worse than the park's 2. Nothing
# usable from either.
# ---
# func_80026D18 MATCHED 2026-08-03, on a pair of shapes neither of which works
# alone. The old entry was accurate as far as it went -- 4 differences, the
# loop counter and accumulator holding each other's register, nine shapes
# including the declaration order all at 4. What it could not see is that the
# two levers are *coupled*:
#   * `do { } while (0);` around the `if` body: 4 -> 2. Not a block -- `{ }`
#     and `if (1) { }` are both 6. gcc's loop pass sees the extra loop node
#     and numbers the blocks differently. The permuter found this one.
#   * `count = 0;` initialised before `i = 0;`: alone it is *worse* (4 -> 6),
#     which is why nine earlier shapes rejected it. With the wrapper it
#     matches.
# A shape measured alone and found worse is not eliminated: two levers can
# only pay together, and the permuter breaks that deadlock because it mutates
# from a base that already carries one of them.
# ---
# func_80073220 clears D_800EAE88[0..5] backwards and then copies the entries
# of D_800F5BE8 that are >= 0xB into it. Every block is right, including the
# dead `if (i != 0)` guard retail emits on a variable it has just zeroed, which
# `while (i <= b[0xA3])` reproduces by itself. Two things do not come out:
# retail materialises the D_800F5BE8 address into $v1 and *copies* it to $a2 --
# 32 instructions against our 31 -- and it holds count in $a1 where we get $a2.
# Nine declaration orders, two-pointer forms for the copy, and the sweep (best
# O1 G0 at 17, which fills a load delay by luck and loses the scheduling of
# i++) all leave the same rotation. `s32 v` rather than `u8 v` for the loaded
# byte is needed for retail's signed `slti`: a u8 local compares in QImode,
# which is unsigned, and that one is worth keeping.
# ---
# func_8004BBBC MATCHED 2026-08-03 and is out of the park. It had been the
# standing example of "one dup-%hi candidate a file flag cannot satisfy": the
# two D_8009B458 reads want the bare form and D_8009AF80 wants cc1psx's own
# split pair hoisted into $s1, and -mno-split-addresses is per *file*, so it
# could only ever give one of them. The resolution is that the choice is
# per *symbol* after all -- a scalar declaration makes cc1psx emit the bare
# symbol for that one name, and the assembler's -G decides whether it is
# expanded, while an unsized array keeps the split pair whatever -G says. The
# parked candidate had been forcing D_8009B458_IS_AGGREGATE, which was the
# whole problem. Default compiler flags, `as -G0`, no source change beyond
# dropping that define.
# ---
# func_8005C4F0 is sixteen instructions and every block is right: `if (v != 0)
# { if (v != -1) return 0; } return -1;`, where retail's `-1` in the first
# branch's delay slot is the *comparison* operand scheduled early, not a return
# value -- writing it as two `return -1`s costs six more differences. The -1
# also has to stay one pseudo shared by the compare and the return; `v == 0 ||
# v == -1` folds the pair into `sltiu v+1,2`.
# What is left is the usual tie: retail keeps v in $v1 and the -1 in $v0, we
# get v in $a0 and the -1 in $v1. Whichever pseudo is allocated first takes
# $v1, and nothing source-side chooses. Sweep best 4, same as O2 G8.
# ---
# func_8004A764 fills five fields of D_8009B458[0] from D_80011434[arg0]. Every
# instruction from the prologue on matches; the nine differences are one block's
# order, because gcc hoists `lui %hi(D_8009B458)` to the top of the function and
# schedules the matching `lw` away from it, where retail keeps the pair adjacent.
# -mno-split-addresses does keep it adjacent but then turns the *table* access
# into an indexed bare-symbol load (`lui $at; addu $at; lw %lo($at)`) instead of
# retail's la + addu + lw, which is worse. Four source orders for the two reads
# (pointer first, value first, address-only in a variable) give 9, 12 and 14.
# ---
# func_8004A6F8 is func_8004A764 with three fields copied from arg1 instead of
# constants, and it sits on the same barrier at 8. It is here for the one thing
# that moved: splitting the table read into an address and a value --
# `s32 *t = &D_80011434[arg0]; u8 *p = D_8009B458[0]; s32 v = *t;` -- took it
# from 26 to 8, because the address computation then lands before the pointer
# load the way retail has it. The same split makes func_8004A764 *worse* (9 to
# 14), so the two are not one problem despite looking like one; each keeps its
# own candidate.
# **Permuter, 2026-08-03: func_8004A6F8 9 -> 6** by dropping the
# `s32 *t = &D_80011434[arg0];` local and reading the table inline -- the
# reverse of what this entry recommends. The entry was right when written:
# the split helped under the *aggregate* addressing both used before the
# dup-%hi retraction. A measurement is only valid against the flags it was
# taken under. func_8004A764's own run found nothing (best 10 against its 6).
# Re-measured 2026-08-03 against the retraction of the split-versus-bare
# "barrier" (see func_8004BBBC). "Retail reaches D_8009B458 with one register
# and gcc uses a separate temp" was that barrier, and the fix is per symbol:
# both candidates were forcing D_8009B458_IS_AGGREGATE, and neither function
# has any %gp_rel, so the plain scalar arm plus `as -G0` gives retail's
# one-register form. func_8004A764 9 -> 6 and func_8004A6F8 8 -> 9, and both
# now have **equal lengths**, which they did not before -- the -1 they each
# carried was the missing second half of the bare load. The flags are in
# build.py's parked section so the counts reproduce.
# What is left is scheduling, and it is the same shape in both: retail
# completes the D_80011434 table address (`sll` then `addu`) *before* loading
# the D_8009B458 pointer, and we load the pointer first. The address/value
# split that helped func_8004A6F8 under the old form now makes func_8004A764
# worse again (6 -> 9), so the two really are separate problems.
# ---
# func_8005C5D4 spins on D_8009B062 for 0x7FFFFFFF iterations, then calls
# func_8008FBE0(1) if it timed out. D_8009B062 has to be volatile or gcc commons
# the loop's read with the entry guard and propagates the flag as zero, which
# deletes the test; that is in variables.h now and it is the useful part.
# Two things remain, and the first is a *tool* difference rather than a source
# one, so read this before trying again:
#   - the 0x7FFFFFFF is materialised by retail as `lui $v1,0x8000` +
#     `addiu $v1,$v1,-1` (splat named the pair D_7FFFFF and wrote 0x7FFFFF for
#     it in undefined_syms_auto.txt, which does not match the bytes -- the
#     encodings are 3C038000 and 2463FFFF). cc1psx emits `li $v1,0x7fffffff`
#     and maspsx's expand_load_immediate turns that into `lui 0x7FFF` +
#     `ori 0xFFFF`. Same value, different bytes. aspsx evidently splits it the
#     %hi/%lo way whenever the low half has bit 15 set. This is the only
#     in-scope function that exercises it, which is why it has never shown up:
#     when such a constant *looks* like an address splat names it and writing
#     `&D_x` reproduces the bytes by accident.
#   - retail's loop is not rotated -- `bgtz` at the head with the timeout call
#     as the fall-through -- and gcc rotates it and drops the first test because
#     the initial value is a known constant. while(1) with the exit inside,
#     a rotated while, a goto loop and an explicit block order with the body
#     behind a forward goto all produce the same 19: gcc's jump pass inverts the
#     branch and lays the call block last whatever the source says.
# ---
# func_80047A68 ORs a bit per slot whose count has reached the limit and hands
# the mask to func_80076ED0. **It needs O1 G0** -- at O2 gcc reverses the loop
# into a countdown from 3 and the whole tail moves, which is 23 differences;
# under O1 G0 the loop, the mask and the call are all exact and only the
# prologue block is left, at 6. `u32 i`, a `for`, and `i = mask` as the
# initialiser do not stop the reversal at O2, so do not spend time there again.
# What is left at O1 G0: retail loads the D_8009B45C pointer into $v0 and
# *copies* it to $v1, which also fills the load delay before `lhu 4($v1)`; we
# load straight into $v1 and take a nop, and `sw $ra` lands first instead of
# after the copy. A second pointer variable does not produce the copy -- gcc
# coalesces the two -- and declaring the bit mask before the accumulator makes
# it 7.
# ---
# func_80021810 walks 0x2D2 halfwords of a 1460-byte record and returns the
# index where the running sum passes a random limit.
# Sibling: func_800243F4 runs the identical pick -- same
# `(func_8008E590() & 0x7FF) + 1`, same running total of halfword weights --
# over D_801781D8, which is D_8017878C minus one 1460-byte tier. Decode either
# and the other follows. Everything from the call
# on is exact; the eleven differences are the multiply chain. Retail computes
# `arg0 * 1460` step by step *into* $s0 -- the callee-saved register the pointer
# lives in, saved before the first shift -- and we compute it in $v0/$v1 and
# copy. Naming the offset, integer-cast address arithmetic with the chain as the
# first operand, and accumulating in place all leave it at 11; declaring the
# locals before assigning them costs 26 more. The sweep agrees at 11.
# ---
# func_80047FAC is ONE instruction and the closest miss in the park. Retail
# zeroes its counter and its accumulator independently -- `addu $s1,$zero,$zero`
# then `addu $s0,$zero,$zero` -- and gcc commons the constant and copies the
# second from the first. Six initialisation shapes (both declaration orders,
# initialiser vs assignment, a `for` header, the pointer hoisted out of the
# loop) all leave exactly that one `addu`, and the sweep's best is the same 1.
# Everything else is exact, including the two levers it needed: the macro flags
# for retail's one-register D_8009B45C load inside the loop, and `p += i * 2;`
# so the base is the addu's destination rather than the scaled index.
# ---
# func_8003D46C MATCHED 2026-08-03, with func_8004BBBC and for the same
# reason. It was the second instance of that "barrier", roles reversed: an
# assembler-expanded bare *store* (`lui $at,%hi(D_8009B34D)` / `sb %lo($at)`)
# next to a cc1psx split *load* of D_800EF6EA into two registers. The old
# entry ended "a sized array declaration does not restore the split pair under
# the macro flags, so the size hint is not a fourth addressing form" -- and
# the error is in the clause after the comma. The size hint was only ever
# tested *with* -mno-split-addresses, which forces every symbol in the file
# bare; with default compiler flags the declaration is per symbol, and the
# assembler's -G then decides only for the ones cc1psx emitted bare. Scalar
# D_8009B34D (the default arm, not the aggregate the candidate was forcing)
# plus `as -G0`, and the split load is untouched because an unsized array is
# never bare.
# **volatile is a real per-symbol escape from the bare form, and it half works
# here.** With -mno-split-addresses plus `volatile u8 D_800EF6EA[]` the store
# comes out exactly right AND the load goes back to a cc1psx pair -- but to
# `lui $v0,%hi(s)` + `addiu $v0,$v0,%lo(s)` + `lbu $a0,0($v0)`, the address
# completed in a register, because volatile also blocks folding %lo into the
# memory operand. Retail folds it. So the knob is worth reaching for when the
# target itself shows the *unfolded* form, and not otherwise; here it is 32
# against 26 and 28.
# ---
# func_80044F58 fills a four-byte packet and then writes three fields through
# D_8009B45C. Under the macro flags everything matches except the tail: retail
# emits the second pointer load *between* the 0x49 store and the 0x510 store,
# so the 0x510 store fills its load delay and the two pointers live in $v0 and
# $v1; gcc keeps one register, stores both fields, then reloads into $v0. A
# second name for the second read and a named second pointer both leave it at
# 9 and 10. The if/else polarity below it is settled -- retail branches out on
# `!= 0`, so the `== 0` arm is written first.
# ---
# func_8003D03C is a dup-%hi candidate that the macro flags do not finish.
# D_8009B408 is touched three times -- read signed, cleared, read unsigned --
# and gcc forms the address once into a register and reaches all three through
# it, unfolded (`lui`+`addiu`+`lb 0($v1)`) where retail has three separate
# folded bare accesses. Two extra names make it *worse* (38 to 40), which is
# the opposite of what they do for func_80047EC4, so the alias rule does not
# reach this shape: there the commoning was loop-invariant motion, here it is
# plain address CSE inside one block.
# ---
# func_80049AF4 is seven differences and all seven are $s0 against $s1: retail
# puts arg0 in $s0 and the constant 1 -- hoisted because it is stored twice --
# in $s1, and gcc does the reverse. Naming the 1 as a local makes it 11.
# ---
# func_8004B854 is three, and they are which instruction fills the call's delay
# slot. Retail emits the three constant arguments, then `lui $a3,%hi(cb)`, then
# the call with `addiu $a3,$a3,%lo(cb)` in the slot -- the split_address_across
# _call shape. gcc emits the callback address first and fills the slot with the
# 0x1000 argument instead, so the post-pass cannot fire: it only rewrites
# `la / call / nop`, and there is no nop to rewrite. Hoisting the 0x1000 into a
# local does not stop gcc sinking it to the call site. Do NOT add this one to
# LA_CALL_FUNCS -- it was tried, the entry is dead, and it was removed again.
# ---
# func_80049434 is twenty stores into D_8009B458[0] and nothing else. Written
# with the symbol inline in every statement it reloads the pointer per store,
# which is right for most of them but not all: retail reuses one load across
# *groups* -- 0x510 with 0x814, then 0x512/0x514/0x516 with it too, while
# 0x815 gets its own. So some runs of stores were one pointer variable in the
# source and some were not, and the grouping has to be recovered store by
# store. 62 differences, all of them that. Worth finishing; just not quick.
# ---
# func_80044E90 is TWO instructions and the shape is fully recovered. Retail
# copies arg0 into $a1 in the first branch's delay slot and uses the copy for
# both `-0x80 - arg0` subtractions while testing the original -- the
# "second variable covering part of the function" shape, and without it there
# is no copy at all and the whole tail shifts (49 differences). With it we get
# the copy and the mirror assignment: retail subtracts $a1 and tests $a0, we
# subtract $a0 and test $a1. Three placements of the two names give the same 2,
# and the sweep agrees.
# ---
# func_80045054 sums the squares of 0x100 halfwords into a field of
# D_8009B45C[0]. It reaches the symbol four times and retail materialises it
# four times, so it needs Base2/Base3/Base4 on top of the macro flags: that is
# 49 differences down to 29, and it is the corrected alias rule working at
# scale. What is left is register allocation around the call result -- retail
# keeps it in $v0 and the pointer in $v1; splitting the reused local into two
# variables makes it 37, so the reuse is not the cause.
# --- the mult/div population, opened 2026-07-27 when that drop rule was
# retracted. Both of these are ordinary C; neither needs an idiom.
# func_8004A27C is 11: the two multiplies, the two zero stores and the call all
# match, and what is left is the %hi temp register plus where the 0xF store
# lands relative to the table read. Hoisting the table read into a local took it
# 16 -> 11; the macro flags make it 25, so this one wants the split pair.
# func_8004A3BC is 25 and needs reading again rather than nudging: the three-way
# `b < 0x40` / `b == 0x40` / `b > 0x40` split with the shared `return 0` is
# right in shape but the negation (`negu` on one path, plain mult on the other)
# and the s16 return conversion are not falling where retail has them.
# func_80047864 is TWO instructions and both are the same one: retail reads
# D_8009B45C with `lui $a0,%hi` + `lw $a0,%lo($a0)` -- one register, the bare
# form -- and we use a separate temp. The macro flags fix exactly that and cost
# five elsewhere (the 0x100000 constant moves from $v1 to $v0 and the `3` store
# reorders behind it), and declaring the constant first does not move it. So
# this one is the split-versus-bare barrier again, at its smallest yet: two
# instructions, one symbol, no second symbol to conflict with -- which makes it
# the best specimen in the park for anyone attacking that class.
# What did get it to 2, and is worth reusing: naming the two derived pointers
# (`q = p + arg0 * 2; r = p + arg0;`) so the base is the addu's first operand.
# Written inline the index comes first and it is 8.
# func_80033CF8 pushes a polar primitive onto the list at D_8009B310 and bumps
# the counter at D_8009B314. Three differences and they are the position of the
# *second* read of D_8009B314 -- retail schedules the `lw` up between two of the
# stores, we leave it at the end. Two levers got it from 40 to 3 and both are
# reusable: reading D_8009B310 inline in the first store (not into a variable
# beforehand) keeps the pointer load *after* the call instead of alive across it,
# which alone was 34 differences and one extra callee-saved register; and naming
# the u16 read of D_8009B314 gets the `lhu` into retail's place. Moving the
# second read between the stores by hand makes it 12, and the sweep agrees at 3.
# func_8002A6B8 turns two signed byte counters into a score: `(a/10)*200 +
# (a%10)*10`, plus 100 if the second is >= 10, plus its own remainder, clamped
# at 0x2D3. The instruction *sequence* is right and it is 41 differences, all
# from one thing worth writing down: retail sign-extends the **quotient** back
# to s8 (`sll 24; sra 24`) before the *200 chain, and with `s8 q = a / 10;` gcc
# omits that extension -- it can see the value fits -- and reorders the
# remainder chain ahead of it. So the quotient in retail's source went through
# something gcc could not see the range of. Needs re-reading with that in mind,
# not nudging.
# func_80018CF8 copies five signed bytes into a local s16[5], then walks the
# slots looking for the record whose field equals k for k = 0x11..0x15, marking
# each hit with -1. The block structure is fully recovered (the goto form in the
# candidate matches retail's blocks one for one) and it is 37 differences, of
# which two things are diagnosed and the rest is the register cascade behind
# them:
#   - `t = (u8 *)D_8015C424;` has to be assigned *after* the first loop, not at
#     its declaration, or the address materialises before the loop: 46 -> 38.
#   - retail keeps 0x48000 in its own register and 0x39FC as the load offset;
#     written as `+ 0x48000 + 0x39FC` gcc folds them into one constant and uses
#     offset 0. Holding 0x48000 in an `s32` variable keeps them apart (37) but
#     is a guess at the source's shape -- 0x8015C424 + 0x48000 + 0x39FC is
#     0x801A7E20, which is D_801A7AD8 + 0x348, so the real source may name a
#     different symbol entirely and that is the thread to pull.
# func_80070650 runs the dispatch table at D_800916E0 until the entry it just
# called is one of three handlers. The shape is right in outline and it is 42
# differences, with one thing measured: retail hoists *two* of the three handler
# addresses into callee-saved registers and materialises the third inside the
# loop, using four callee-saved registers where we use five. Everything after
# the prologue shifts by that one register. The three comparisons are written as
# a value read once into a local (retail reloads the table entry after the call
# and compares it three times, so that part is right).
func_80070650
    RESOLVED -- src/func_80070650.c is in the build and the image hashes. Entry kept for its diagnosis, which is the record of what the lever turned out to be.

# func_80012AE8 -- 26/24, 26 differences, and the diagnosis is now settled: it
# is NOT cc1psx output at all. Re-tried 2026-08-21 and the C is entirely
# reachable. The count `lui $s1,(0x0>>16); addiu $s1,$s1,0x0` is a %hi/%lo
# pair on a symbol the linker resolves to zero, and `config/symbol_aliases.txt`
# is exactly the mechanism for that: `CtorCount_0 = 0x0;` plus
# `extern u8 CtorCount_0[];` and `n = (s32)CtorCount_0;` emits the pair (gcc
# materialises a plain 0 as `addu $s1,$zero,$zero`, so no literal reaches it).
# With that, `as -G0` for D_800906E0 and D_80010000_IS_AGGREGATE, every
# instruction of the body matches in order.
# What does not is the FRAME. Retail's is 0x10 with $s0/$s1/$ra at 4/8/0xC and
# **no outgoing-argument area at all**, and its epilogue puts `addiu $sp,$sp,16`
# *before* `jr $ra` with a `nop` in the delay slot. A two-line control probe
# settles it: cc1psx compiling `void f(void) { func_8004A2F8(); }` -- one call,
# no arguments -- reserves 16 bytes anyway and comes out at 24, and it always
# sinks the stack adjust into the `jr`'s delay slot. No row of sweep_try
# changes either (best 23, at -O1 with the delay-slot macro). So this is
# hand-written assembly or a different toolchain's object, most likely the
# PsyQ startup's constructor walker, and it should be left as assembly.

    A stored permuter output measures 15 against 16; not yet decomposed.
    **16 -> 15**, marginal, and still one instruction short (23 against 24) --
    the base was too, so the shortfall is older than this change. The shape is
    `if (p) { four stores } else { the same four stores; return; }` with the
    0x1000 stores after it. Inert: p is `&D_800F2C40[arg0 * 3616]`, the address
    of an array element, so it is never null, `if (p)` is always taken and the
    else arm is dead. Same family as func_8005A3D0's double `break` -- a
    control-flow node with no runtime cost. Verify p cannot be null before
    reusing this shape anywhere else; here that check is what made it safe.

    one (11 -> 7). Reusing a local for two unrelated values swaps the pair.

func_8001EE44_WAS -- 22 positional differences, two registers and the scheduling
  that follows from them.
  Extracts a 4-bit field from D_801D4244[record->unkC - 1] twice, once per
  argument, choosing shift 18 or 22 on the 0x200 flag at record+0x16, and
  passes both to func_8002CB80. Everything matches but this: retail leaves the
  shift result in $v0 and writes the argument register at the mask
  (`sra $v0,$v0,18` ... `andi $a0,$v0,15`), while gcc coalesces the shift
  straight into $a0 and then masks in place. The knock-on is one instruction of
  scheduling: with the mask in place, gcc can sink it into the following
  `beq`'s delay slot, and retail puts %hi(D_801D4244) there instead.
  The mask must be its own statement after the if/else -- inside the call
  arguments gcc sinks it past the second block entirely (24). Beyond that,
  every attempt to split the unmasked value into its own local costs a
  register: gcc then copies arg1 into $a2 and the count goes to 52. Tried the
  conditional-expression form (which is what fixed func_800595C8's identical
  looking symptom), `a = a & 0xF` versus `a &= 0xF`, u32 locals, and both
  declaration orders -- all 22. Full flag sweep: nothing below 22.

func_800383DC_WAS -- 42 positional differences, one instruction short, and the
  missing instruction is an optimisation retail did not take.
  Builds a far pointer as `(base & 0xFFFF0000) + table[index]` and appends it
  to the u32 array at arg0, indexed by the byte counter at arg0+0x58. Three
  ranges of D_8009B32E pick the table: > 0xCFFF uses D_801C0000 at v - 0xD000,
  > 0x7FFF uses D_801D5800 at v - 0x8000 (these two share the tail), and the
  rest uses D_801C0000 at v (minus 0x100 above 0x500) with D_801B0000 as the
  masked base.
  Block layout, the shared tail, the branch that falls into it, and the whole
  low branch all match. The one fault: in the D_801C0000 arm gcc computes
  `(u32)D_801C0000 & 0xFFFF0000` as the `lui %hi(D_801C0000)` it already has,
  and so never materialises 0xFFFF0000 there. Retail emits `lui $a0,65535` in
  *both* arms and does the `and` at the join. The fold only fires on the symbol
  whose low half is zero -- D_801D5800 gets the real constant in both builds --
  which is what makes it look like a register difference until you count.
  Getting to 42: `s32 v`, not `u16` (a u16 makes every comparison `sltu`;
  retail's are `slt`) -- 49 to 43; and the low arm reached by a `goto` placed
  *after* the shared tail, which is where retail puts it -- 43 to 42.
  Tried at 42: tbl as u32 rather than u8*, a separate `base` local for the
  masked value, both assignment orders inside the arms, both declaration
  orders, and -mno-split-addresses (45, and it loses the `addu` as well).
  Flag sweep: -O2 -G0 reports 23 but only because it also drops the gp-relative
  load of D_8009B32E, which is two more faults, not fewer.

func_8002A3CC_WAS -- 26 differences, register allocation driving the schedule.
  Advances a 16.16 fixed-point position held as two halfword pairs in
  D_800EA1E8 (+0x8/+0xC is x, +0xA/+0xE is y, deltas at +0x18/+0x1C), counts
  down the byte at +0x16, and on reaching zero snaps back to +0x12/+0x14,
  clears the active flag at +0x17 and calls func_8003FEE0(0x35) and
  func_8002A2F4. Returns the flag byte, which the early-out path gets from the
  entry load by CSE -- that part matches.
  The fault is one register and everything the scheduler does behind it. Retail
  builds each 32-bit value into $a2 (`or $a2,$v0,$v1`), which keeps it live
  while the second pair's three loads are hoisted above the first pair's
  stores; we build it into $v0 in place, gcc sees $v0 die at the `or`, and the
  stores stay put. Same instructions, interleaved differently, and the two
  pairs' registers rotate with it.
  Tried: both stores after both computations (26), stores in the other order
  within each pair (27), named locals for the two halves (46), `x = hi|lo;
  x += delta;` split (43). Flag sweep: nothing below 26; -mno-split-addresses
  is 34.

    the 0x100 constant, which then leaves a `nop` after the 0x18 load;
  - retail leaves a `nop` after `lw $v0,0x20($s1)` and puts `sw $zero,0xC($s0)`
    in the slot before the call; we put the 0xC store in the load's slot.
  Tried: the 0x60 store moved after the 0x18 pair (29) and before the 0x48
  pair (50), the 0xC store moved after the 0x44 copy (50). Flag sweep: 22 is
  the floor, and -fno-schedule-insns does not help -- retail's schedule is not

func_8005C388 MATCHED 2026-08-30 by PORTING Unchiga's C (docs/MERGE_UNCHIGA.md);
  the history below is what this tree had reached on its own.
func_8005C388_WAS -- 11 differences, register assignment around the table base.
  Reads a halfword pair out of D_8009151C at arg0*4, sums the first halfword of
  every earlier pair, and hands the result to func_8005B8A0 through a
  func_8007E600/func_8007E710 pair. Instruction-for-instruction right; retail
  keeps the base in $a1 and the running index in $v1, we get the base in $v1
  and the loop pointer in $a0, and gcc hoists the loop pointer's initialisation
  above the counter's where retail has it after.
  44 -> 11, and each step is a lever already in WORKFLOW.md:
  - the base needs a local, or cc1psx folds the first reference's `+2` into
    `%lo(D_8009151C+2)` and every later offset is 2 too small (4 differences,
    but the wrong 4);
  - that local must be **scoped to before the calls**, or it goes callee-saved
    and costs a register everywhere (44);
  - the loop needs its own pointer variable: retail's `addu $v1,$a1,$zero` is
    a copy, and a copy is a second name (36 -> 18);
  - and that pointer must be assigned *at* the loop, not at its declaration
    (18 -> 11).
  Also: the provisional prototype said `u8 *arg0`; arg0 indexes the table, so
  it is an s32. Corrected in functions.h.

func_80017F04 -- 12 differences: two registers and the scheduling behind them.
  Creates a sprite for the record at arg0 and fills five bytes at +0x67..+0x6B,
  one of which is the record's index computed as a pointer difference divided
  by 28 (gcc factors that into `srl 2` plus the unsigned magic multiply for 7).
  Retail puts the magic constant in $a0 and the D_801D4244 base in $v0; we get
  $v1 for both, and the two `sb $zero` stores then schedule ahead of the
  `lh 0xC($s1)` instead of into the gaps after it.
  47 -> 12 with a base local for D_801D4244 assigned before the +0x68
  statement. Tried at 12 or worse: five orderings of the four byte stores
  (the division first is 20, the zero stores last 51).
  Worth recording separately: this function needs a **per-file prototype**.
  It has three parameters -- arg1/arg2 go to the sprite's +0x30/+0x32 -- but
  func_80018004 calls it with one and retail sets neither $a1 nor $a2 there,
  so that translation unit never saw the real declaration. functions.h now
  guards it on FUNC_80017F04_FULL, which the definition's file defines and
  nobody else does. First function prototype in the project to need the same
  per-file treatment the variables have.
  Re-measured 2026-08-03. The candidate had stopped compiling: it carried its
  own one-parameter prototypes for func_80016778 and func_80016D04, both of
  which have since been decompiled with a second parameter. They are only ever
  stored as function pointers here, so the parameter list is immaterial and the
  fix is to match the real definitions -- but this is the second time a park
  has rotted because the declarations around it moved, and check_try_func's
  parked direction is what caught it. Still 12, and now with equal lengths so
  the count is real.
  Four more spellings of the `- 1` measured against the fold, all 12: a byte
  base with an explicit `* 4`, the constant grouped before the base, `(tbl -
  1)[n]`, and `&tbl[n - 1]` as its own element pointer. gcc distributes the
  `- 1` into the load's displacement every time, which costs one instruction
  and gains a `nop` -- net zero, which is why the length matches. Naming the
  index as a local *costs* an instruction (47) whether declared at the top or
  scoped to its own block. The fold is a symptom of the register choice the
  entry already names, not a separate lever.

func_8005F1B8 MATCHED 2026-08-30 by PORTING Unchiga's C (docs/MERGE_UNCHIGA.md);

func_8005F1B8_WAS -- 24 differences, and most of them hang off one thing this
  project has not seen before: **retail divides by a literal constant with a
  real `div`**, materialising 750 into $a0 first, where gcc turns `/ 750` into
  the magic multiply at every -O level (0, 1 and 2 all use it here).
  Structure otherwise recovered: `if (arg0 < 2)` with the body inside rather
  than an early return (30 -> 24, the same polarity lesson as func_8003FD14),
  the 0x32 clamp, the -0x12C, the `(s16)` test, and the halve-if-positive.
  The class is small and clustered: four in-scope functions divide by a
  register that was just loaded with a constant, and three of them
  (func_8005F1B8, func_8005F27C, func_8005F5C8) are neighbours, so it is
  probably one translation unit built differently rather than a source shape.
  Probed and rejected: -O0/-O1/-O2 (all magic), -fno-expensive-optimizations,
  -fno-peephole, -msoft-float. `-mno-mult` and `-mint64` are not accepted by
  cc1psx. Whatever turns the magic off is not in the flags tried so far, and
  worth another look when a fifth example turns up.

func_8003C568 -- 38 differences, one scheduling decision and the operand order

  the `v = D_8009B0F4; v &= mask; D_8009B0F4 = v;` that case 0 needs**
  (45 -> 15). One arm of a switch wanting the local form while three others
  want the direct form is the two-names rule at arm granularity, and it is
  worth 30 differences here.

  retail materialises the 0xFFDCFFFF mask before the two-instruction 0x18000

func_8003D03C -- 10 differences, and the -G threshold gets the *addressing*
  right and the *schedule* wrong. Recorded because it is the first case where
  the new lever is measurably worse than the old one.
  Retail materialises %hi(D_8009B408) three separate times -- $v1 for a `lb`,
  $at for a `sb`, $v0 for a `lbu` -- which is the bare form, one expansion per
  reference. Declaring it `[8]` and assembling at -G4 produces exactly that
  (D_8009B3B8 stays gp-relative at four bytes), and the count goes *up*, from
  10 to 39: every bare load then carries a `nop` in its load-delay slot,
  because the bare reference is one pseudo-instruction when cc1psx schedules
  and maspsx only expands it afterwards, so nothing is available to fill.
  Retail fills that slot with the `sw $zero,0x600(arg0)` that follows.
  That toolchain-gap hypothesis is now **checked and mis-framed**: maspsx has a
  `nop_at_expansion` knob, but it *inserts* a nop before an `$at` expansion --
  the opposite of filling a slot -- and neither maspsx nor the real aspsx
  reschedules at all. Scheduling is cc1psx's, so the empty slot is a compiler
  decision about a reference it treats as one instruction, not an assembler
  one. Whatever is left here, patching maspsx is not it.
  Original note, kept because the alternative in it is still open: not
  proven -- it is equally possible retail's unit reached the symbol some way
  neither route reproduces. Left on the old route (aggregate, default flags,
  10) rather than the new one.

func_80048920 MATCHED 2026-08-30 by PORTING Unchiga's C (docs/MERGE_UNCHIGA.md);

func_80048920_WAS -- 20 differences, a $s1/$s2 swap and the two operand orders that
  fall out of it. Worth reading for the lever that got it there.
  Retail builds `(arg0 & 0x100) != 0` into a register and shifts it:
  `andi $v1,$a0,256 / sltu $v1,$zero,$v1 / sll $v1,$v1,6`. Every one-expression
  spelling of that -- `(x & 0x100) != 0` shifted, multiplied by 64, `!!`, a
  ternary, an unsigned `0 < ...` -- lets gcc combine the whole thing into
  `srl $v1,$s1,2 / andi $v1,$v1,64`, two instructions where retail has three,
  and the shortfall cascades through the rest of the function (57, and the
  ternary 72). **Splitting it across two statements against the same variable**
  --- `f = arg0 & 0x100; f = f != 0;` --- blocks the combine and reproduces
  retail exactly: 57 differences to 20. Same shape as the two-names rule, in
  the opposite direction: one name reused is what stops a fold here.
  What is left: retail keeps the pre-mask value in $a2 (caller-saved, dead
  before the loop) and the masked id in $s2, with arg1 in $s1. We get one live
  range in $s1 and arg1 in $s2. Splitting it into two source variables is the
  obvious fix and makes it *worse* (65) in all four spellings tried: a separate
  `v` initialised at its declaration, assigned as the first statement, assigned
  after the guard, and reassigning the parameter itself.
  2026-08-11: first permuter run (30 min) on this function, 20 -> 18 with two
  inert names decomposed and verified: the scaled index (arg0 & 0x1F) * 2
  named before f's two-statement mask, and a copy of id for the 0xFFFF
  comparison. The $s1/$s2 swap itself did not move.

func_80031CD4_WAS -- 8 differences, one scheduling decision in the first block, and
  it is the first case where the `lui $at` expansion *hides* a load-use hazard
  from the scheduler instead of exposing one.
  Retail loads the halfword into $v1, computes the next address in $v0, and
  only then stores it to D_8009B338 -- the `lh $v0,0x2D3C($s0)` fills the
  `lhu`'s load-delay slot. We put the value and the address in the same $v0, so
  the store follows the load immediately and maspsx's `lui $at` expansion lands
  in the delay slot by accident. Same instruction count, one slot apart, and
  everything after it shifts.

  wants `addu $v0,$s0,$v0` (base first) for `p + n * 16`, which `p[n * 16 + K]`
  does not give and `*(p + (n << 4) + K)` does -- the same spelling that closed
  func_8002A2F4.

  2026-08-21, two measured findings that do not yet compose. (a) `p =
  D_8009B45C;` **re-assigned as the last statement of the loop body**, with
  the `while` reading `*(s16 *)(p + 0x4C)`, is what puts the three case
  constants in retail's $t2/$t1/$t0 and the three induction variables in

  register downstream is shifted by one. It costs 2 instructions, though: gcc

  `slt`/`beqz exit` followed by a redundant **`bgez $a2`** (the counter is
  never negative). The only spelling found that emits it is an explicit
  `if (i < 0) break;` as the loop's last statement, which is +1 -- so the
  shape is identified and the source is not. A full sweep_try finds no flag
  row better than the installed 61.

func_8001944C -- unfinished, 30 differences with equal lengths (70/70).
    MATCHED 2026-08-31 by PORTING Unchiga's C from his tangos/work branch (docs/MERGE_UNCHIGA.md). It compiled to the retail bytes under cc1psx 2.8.1 unchanged -- the park below records what OUR source shape could not reach, and is kept for that.
    Needs the **default compiler with a -G0 assembler**, which is a
    combination the flag sweep could not produce until today: D_8009B0AC is a
    one-byte scalar, small data at -G8, so cc1psx emits one instruction and
    gcc's delay-slot filler hoists it into the preceding branch's slot where
    retail keeps a nop. At as -G0 the same one compiler instruction is a macro
    to the assembler and the nop survives. That row alone was 62 differences
    with a length mismatch to 32 with equal lengths; sweep_flags now carries
    four as-only rows for it.
    Then 32 -> 30 by fixing two offsets (0x8000 + 0x565C is 0xD65C, and I had
    written 0xDC5C) and moving the `b = D_8015C424` assignment after the first
    rectangle store so the two symbols materialise in retail's order.
    What is left is one allocation question that pulls two ways, and it is
    worth reading before spending anything here:
      * Retail copies the table base into a cursor before the loop
        (`addu $a1,$s0,$zero`) *and* re-materialises D_8015C424 after it. With
        one local reused for both, we get the copy and no rematerialisation
        (30). With a separate local for the block after the loop, we get the
        rematerialisation and gcc then walks the first local itself, dropping
        the copy -- 67 instructions, three short. Three loop forms (a forward
        `for` with a cursor, an index expression, `*q++`) all land at 68.
      * The four rectangle stores are 0, 4, 6, 2 in retail. Through the symbol
        gcc reorders them to 4, 6, 0, 2 because it can prove the offsets
        disjoint; through a local pointer the order is retail's, but the local
        then takes $a3 and costs an `addu $a0,$a3,$zero` (+2). The offset-0
        store is `%lo(sym)($hi)` in retail and the others are `n($a0)`, which
        is exactly the mixed shape, so the answer is probably a narrower split
        than "all symbol" or "all local" -- I did not find it.
    Also measured: passing the 0/0x140 value as a third argument to
    func_80081ED4 puts it in $a2 where retail has it (53 from 58), which is
    the "a value left in an argument register is the next call's argument"
    lever pointing the right way, but it then displaces the rectangle pointer
    into $a3. Not installed, because a third argument to a two-argument
    library function is a guess and the length got worse.

func_8003B5C8 -- unfinished, 18 differences and one instruction too many, and
    MATCHED 2026-08-31 by PORTING Unchiga's C from his tangos/work branch (docs/MERGE_UNCHIGA.md). It compiled to the retail bytes under cc1psx 2.8.1 unchanged -- the park below records what OUR source shape could not reach, and is kept for that.
    the whole of it is one induction variable. Twenty bytes of Shift-JIS
    full-width digits are copied from D_80010330 onto the stack, then read as
    ten big-endian halfword keys, each looked up in the four-byte-entry table
    at D_801D9004 and its 1-based position stored to D_800EAFF8.
    Everything matches except the loop bound. Retail eliminates the counter:
    it keeps `a3 = &b[k]` as an address giv, `t0 = k + 1` as a biased biv for
    the second byte, `t1` as the output giv, and tests `slt $a3,$t4` against a
    limit `t4 = sp + 20` computed in the preheader. Note the `slt` -- a
    *signed* compare between two addresses, which is the fingerprint of gcc
    rewriting the original `k < 20` rather than of a pointer comparison in the
    source. We get the same three ivs plus the counter itself, and `slti`.
    Our gcc will not build the address giv for the array the loop **reads**,
    though it builds one for `D_800EAFF8[j]`, which the loop **writes**. The
    first reading of that was "frame-relative access", and it is wrong -- four
    synthetic probes say so, and they cost one try_func run each:
      * the same counted loop over a *global* `u8` array: no giv for the load
        either, so the frame has nothing to do with it;
      * a single read instead of two: same;
      * the exit test moved onto the other counter, so the load's index is not
        the test biv: same;
      * both indices at unit stride: same. In every one, the store's
        destination gets `addiu $reg,$reg,2` and the load's source is
        recomputed with an `addu` each iteration.
    So the measured statement is: **this cc1psx builds an address giv for a
    store's destination and not for a load's source**, and retail's listing
    shows one for a load, which is the thing not yet explained. Not a
    toolchain-version difference either -- 4.6 behaves identically on the probe
    and is 8 instructions worse on the candidate.
    Measured source shapes, all landing worse:
      * `b[k]` and `b[k + 1]` (both spellings of the buffer, struct member and
        plain array): no giv at all, two `addu`s per iteration, 55/57.
      * naming the second index (`m = k + 1` as a loop clause): three
        increments and *equal length*, but a3 holds `k` rather than `&b[k]`,
        so every register in the body rotates -- 42.
      * an explicit cursor for the first byte plus the named index for the
        second: 18 and +1, the entry above. Four increments where retail has
        three.
      * bounding that cursor instead of the counter (`c < b + 20`, and with an
        `end` local): +4 or +5. gcc recomputes `sp + 20` inside the loop and
        compares `sltu`, and the counter's disappearance does not pay for it.
      * indexing through a pointer local (`bp = b; bp[k]`): 33.
    The flag sweep's best is 45 (O2 G8 nosched1+2), which is scheduling noise
    and not this. What would settle it is a second function that indexes a
    stack array by a counted loop variable and whose target *does* show the
    giv; if that one also refuses, the class is the frame-relative access and
    not this function.

func_8003CCD8 -- unfinished, 6 differences with equal lengths (72/72), and all
    six are the order of the last six stores. The controller decoder: 32 bits
    of held state walked from bit 31 down, an auto-repeat counter per button in
    D_800EF6B0, and the result written out as three 32-bit values in halfword
    pairs.
    68 -> 28 -> 6 on addressing alone, and the second step is a **new use of
    the declared-size knob**. D_8009B39C and D_8009B3A2 are gp-relative here,
    so they want the scalar guards. D_8009B0D8 is read one byte at a time
    *inside the loop* and retail re-materialises `lui %hi` every iteration --
    with the unsized-array form cc1psx emits its own %hi/%lo pair and gcc
    hoists the %hi out as a loop invariant, which is one instruction too many
    in the preheader and one too few in the body. The fix is the same
    mechanism as D_80010038's `[4]`: give the symbol a *declared size it does
    not have* (`u8 D_8009B0D8[8]`, guarded on D_8009B0D8_IS_SIZED) and
    assemble at **-G4**. Eight bytes clears the -G4 threshold so the symbol is
    not small data and the assembler expands the bare reference through the
    destination register; the four-byte scalars around it (D_8009B3A8,
    D_8009B390, D_8009B3B0, D_8009B3B4) are `<= 4` and stay gp-relative. And
    because the bare form is *one* instruction to gcc, there is nothing for
    the loop-invariant pass to hoist. -G0/-G1/-G2 all wreck the gp-relative
    group (+20, +18, +12); -G4 is the only threshold that separates them.
    What is left: retail stores the three pairs in source order --
    `sh lo; sh hi` for each of D_8009B3A4/6, D_8009B398/9A, D_8009B394/6 --
    and we sink all three `lo` stores past the `hi` stores, which all reuse
    $v0 from their own `srl`. Measured against it, none of these moves it:
      * writing the pairs hi-first (8, worse);
      * naming each shifted half in a local, one name reused or three separate
        names (both 6, byte-identical to the inline form);
      * declaring each pair as one two-element `u16` array so the stores are
        to a single object (7 -- gcc reorders stores within one object too,
        so aliasing is not the lever here);
      * declaring the two low halves **volatile**, using the guards that
        already exist for them: **byte-identical output**. Worth knowing on
        its own -- WORKFLOW says gcc sinks a non-volatile store past a
        volatile one, and here the volatile store is the one that moves. Do
        not reach for volatile to pin a store order on the strength of that
        rule alone.
      * -fno-schedule-insns (+3, 43), -fno-schedule-insns2 (15), both (+7).

func_80047788_WAS -- unfinished, 12 differences with equal lengths (55/55).
    Another from the reopened dup-%hi class: three bare reads of
    D_8009B45C, no %gp_rel, plain scalar arm plus `as -G0` (in build.py's
    parked section). 48 -> 18 -> 12 with two source levers, both worth
    reusing.
    First, **gcc folds `(x * 8 + 0x7FF) / 2048` into `(x + 255) / 256`**
    and retail does not, so the whole prologue was wrong by two
    instructions. An intermediate assignment blocks the fold:
    `n = *(u16 *)(p + 2) * 8;` then `n = (n + 0x7FF) / 2048 + 1;`. Writing
    it as one expression, with `<< 3` instead of `* 8`, or splitting the
    `+ 0x7FF` into its own statement instead, all keep the fold or cost
    more (48, 48, 22).
    Second, the tail's address wants its own name. Written as one
    expression -- `*(s32 *)(off + *(u8 **)(p + 0x448) + 4)` -- it is one
    instruction short; reusing `e` for it (`e = off + *(u8 **)(p + 0x448);`
    then `*(s32 *)(e + 4)`) is the missing `addu`. 18 to 12 and equal
    lengths.
    What is left is two `addu` operand orders and one delay slot:
      * **the permuter took this from 12 to 11** (2026-08-03) by splitting
        `e = off + *(u8 **)(p + 0x448);` into the load and the add as two
        statements. The parked candidate carries that now.
      * retail has `addu $v1,$s0,$v1` and `addu $s0,$s0,$v0` -- the offset
        first at both sites, with the *other* operand's register as the
        destination. With `off` a named local we get the base first at
        both. Inlining the index at either site or both changes nothing
        (12 each), and `base + off` in source order is the same 12. This
        is the func_8004DB14 lever pointing the wrong way: there a named
        offset *gave* base-first and that was what retail wanted; here
        retail wants index-first and naming is forced, because the value
        is used again after two calls.
      * retail leaves the `bgez` of the division sequence with a `nop`;
        gcc duplicates the target `sra` into the slot and branches to
        target+4. Naming the quotient separately, naming the biased
        dividend, and four-statement long division all leave it (12, 12,
        23). -fno-schedule-insns / -insns2 / -fno-delayed-branch are all
        worse and change the length.

    scaled offset, and -- the fix for the two missing instructions -- writing
    the first address as TWO statements against one name,
    `e = D_8009B458[0]; e = e + off;`. As one expression gcc folds the 0x180
    into the load displacement (`lbu ...,387`) and flips the `addu`'s operands
    to index-first; split, retail's `addiu $v0,$v0,384` and
    `addu $a1,$a2,$v0` both come back. **A base local re-enables the fold**:
    four spellings with `b = D_8009B458[0];` -- the offset as one expression,
    as two statements, with the base assigned before or after -- are all -2
    again, so the split only works when `e`'s first definition is the load
    itself rather than a copy.
    What is left: retail loads D_8009B458 once into `$a2` and reuses it in
    both blocks; our two symbol reads become two `lw`, and retail has an
    `addu $v0,$v1,$zero` copy before the second multiply that we lack. Those
    two cancel in the instruction count, which is why 23/23 hides them. A
    second name for the byte (`w = v; off = w * 24;`) does not produce the
    copy -- it coalesces, in both the one-expression and two-statement forms.
    The two wants are in tension: the single load needs a base local and the
    base local re-folds the 0x180.

func_80070710_WAS / func_80071424 / func_80071460 -- 8, 3 and 3 differences, all
    with the right length and instruction sequence.
    The two `D_800F5B98[func_8007058C()] = <global>` functions went 6 -> 3 via
    siblings.py, which pairs them at ratio 1.000 and 0.933 with func_80071688
    and func_8007164C -- both already matched, both the same one-liner. The
    matched ones put the table base in $a0 and the value in $v1; these two want
    the reverse. $v1 is allocated before $a0, so the table's pseudo has to be
    created FIRST, and writing the address into its own pointer does it:
    `s32 *p = &D_800F5B98[func_8007058C()]; *p = <global>;`. Plain pointer
    arithmetic and a computed byte address are also 3; a base local for the
    table is 11 and a local for the value symbol is 10, so it is specifically
    the whole address that must be named.
    What is left in both is one scheduling step: retail materialises the table
    before the `sll $v0,$v0,2` that scales the index, and gcc emits the shift
    first. The permuter was run on both from the 3 base and returned 6 and 10.
    Cross-checked against gonzaloberteri/ygofm-decomp, which has all three in
    src/manual/ with per-function flag comments. Their C agrees with ours on
    semantics and their annotated flags (-O2 -G0, -fno-schedule-insns2 on two)
    give the same counts as our defaults, so the overlap confirmed the reading
    and moved nothing. Their D_8009B361 is a scalar and ours an array; both
    measure the same.
    One trap from that exercise: `D_800F5B98[...] = D_8009B361;` against our
    array declaration stores the symbol's ADDRESS and measures 4, better than
    the correct `[0]` form at 6. Read the semantics before the count.
    sweep_try.py run 2026-08-10: best is 8 at -O1 -G0, same as the base.
    2026-09-04, ASSEMBLY DEBT RETIREMENT: real C written for it for the first
    time, kept in parked/func_80070710.c. src/ still holds the transcription,
    which matches; this does not, so nothing was swapped. It reaches 10/10 with
    the same 8 register-name differences the entries above record, so the
    residue is confirmed on a fresh derivation rather than inherited.
    Two spellings ADDED to the six already listed, both predicted to work by
    WORKFLOW's "a symbol added last is emitted last; a base local is how you
    move it" rule, and both worth nothing:
      - `u8 *t = D_800917F0;` assigned FIRST, then `s32 i = D_8009B361[0];`
      - the same with the index inline, and an s32 variant added as integers
    All eight spellings are byte-identical to each other. The rule does not
    reach this function, which is worth knowing because the diff LOOKS exactly
    like an ordering problem: the target materialises the table first and we
    materialise the index first.
    The sharper reading of the columns, which none of the earlier entries
    states: the target spends ONE register on the index -- `lui $a0,%hi` then
    `lb $a0,%lo(...)($a0)` into the same register -- and we spend two, `$v0`
    for the %hi and `$a0` for the loaded byte. That extra live value is what
    pushes the table out of `$v0` into `$v1`, and every downstream register
    follows. So the question is not which symbol is materialised first; it is
    why gcc will not land the byte in the register that held its own %hi.
    Permuter restarted from this base (score 65) rather than from the
    transcription, which is the base Unchiga's 2.5M iterations used.

    `D_8009B458[0][(arg0 & 0xFF) * 24 + 4]`.
    D_8009B458 needs the aggregate arm, and the base is RE-READ every
    iteration -- same shape as func_8004CA60, and the pre-loop read is what the
    first iteration uses.
    74 -> 63 differences by initialising the loop counter FIRST and deriving
    the other two zeros from it (`i = 0; mask = i; ... off = i;`) rather than
    writing three separate zeros: retail materialises zero once and copies it
    twice, `addu $s1,$s0,$zero` and `addu $s2,$s0,$zero`. Length did not move.
    Retail's allocation is s6=arg0, s5=arg1, s4=arg0&0xFF, s3=q, s2=off,
    s1=mask, s0=i. Ours adds an eighth; which value is spilling into it is not
    yet identified. The permuter has not been run.
    The extra register is identified: gcc hoists `arg1 & 0xFF` out of the loop
    into a callee-saved, while retail recomputes it every iteration in the
    first `bne`'s delay slot (`andi $v0,$s5,255`) and keeps only raw arg1.
    Retail does hoist `arg0 & 0xFF`, which is reused by the final store, so
    exactly one of the two masks is loop-invariant to it and both are to us.
    Measured and all still 76/63: naming the masked value in a local inside the
    loop body, `u8 arg1`, and `u8 arg0, u8 arg1` with the masks dropped.
    The permuter reached 75/48 and its candidate is SEMANTICALLY WRONG -- it
    pins a second base name before the loop, never updates it, and reads the
    `while` bound from that stale pointer, where retail's `lh 0x510` uses the
    base it just re-read. Reconstructing the same shape correctly is 76/63
    again, so the permuter's gain came from the bug. Third broken candidate
    from it today.
    **63 -> 25 and the length fixed, from sweep_try.py, which I had not run.**
    `-mno-split-addresses` (the sweep's "O2 G8 macro" row) takes it to 74/74
    with 25 differences: the bare form frees the register the eighth
    callee-saved was eating, so the two-instruction excess is gone. Everything
    else in the sweep is 63 or worse, and the four as-only -G rows are all 63.
    What is left is prologue ORDER: retail saves $s5 and copies arg1 into it
    before saving $s0 and zeroing the counter, and reads D_8009B458 before the
    frame is even set up. Reading the base before `i = 0` and copying arg1 into
    an early local are both still 25.
    **The per-symbol route reaches the same 25 without any compiler flag**, and
    is what the candidate now carries: the plain SCALAR declaration of
    D_8009B458 plus `as -G0`. There is no %gp_rel in the function, so recipe
    branch 1 applies and any -G is free (-G0 and -G2 both give 25; -G4 is 71
    instructions and 72). This matters because -mno-split-addresses is
    file-wide while D_80011434 wants cc1psx's own pair with a separate temp,
    which the flag takes away -- the declaration is per symbol and the flag is
    not.

    `& 0xFF` from the second comparison, so the code compares $s5 raw where

    else in the 79 matches, including the real `div` that aspsx wraps with

    product to $v1 instead of $v0, which is three differences instead of

func_80049FB4 -- 74 differences, and listed here as a *decode* rather than a
    near miss, so that the reading is not redone. The arithmetic is settled
    and reads back cleanly:
        n = (s16)(arg1 + arg3);  q = n / 128;  r = n % 128;
        d = (s16)(arg0 + q - arg2);
        d >= 0:  return D_80010834[(d % 12) * 128 + r] << (d / 12);
        d <  0:  m = -d;
                 return D_80010834[((12 - m % 12) % 12) * 128 + r]
                        >> ((m + 11) / 12);
    Both `/ 12` are reciprocal multiplies by 0x2AAAAAAB (mfhi, sra 1,
    subtract the sign), so the divisor really was the literal 12 -- the
    opposite of func_8005F27C's `div`, and the two together are the pair
    that makes that rule readable.

    computes the quotient candidate in one register; and retail falls
    through into the d < 0 arm (`bgez -> the positive arm`) where gcc
    inlines the positive one whichever way the source is written.

func_8005F5C8 MATCHED 2026-08-18. The answer was `u = d * t;` into a FRESH
    name followed by `t = u;`: the fresh destination gives retail's (d, t)
    operand order, because expand_binop swaps a commutative operator's
    operands when the destination is one of them, and the copy back links
    the new pseudo to t's register, which is where retail puts the product.
    The copy coalesces away. Either half alone is three differences, which
    is why nine hand shapes and two permuter runs all floored at one.
    What stood here follows.
    ONE difference, and it is the SAME one as func_8005F27C:
    `mult $v1,$v0` against our `mult $v0,$v1`, same registers, operands
    swapped. That matters more than either function alone. The candidate is
    func_8005F27C's, adapted in four lines (a fourth parameter, two extra
    arguments on the tail call, func_8005D994 for func_8005F3B8), and it
    came out 83/83 at one difference on the first compile -- so the residue
    is systematic and not this function's register pressure.
    The mechanism, as far as it is established: gcc's expand_binop swaps a
    commutative operator's operands when the destination is one of them, so
    `t = d * t` and `t = t * d` both emit (t, d) with the result in t's
    register. A fresh destination emits retail's (d, t) and is then
    allocated $v1 -- d's own register, which dies at the multiply -- where
    retail has $v0. Both halves are reachable and they do not compose.
    Measured here as well as on the sibling: fresh name declared first and
    last, the one-expression form, `t = t * d`, and `v = v + d * t / k`.
    Solving it is worth two functions, so it is the best permuter target in
    the park list.

func_800260D0 -- 25 differences, all of them the scheduling of one block:
    the one between func_8002C604 returning and func_8003FEE0 being called.
    Retail evaluates every input of that block first -- %hi/%lo(D_80090800),
    `lh D_8009B20C+2`, `lbu D_8009B1D5` -- and only then stores into the
    record; we build the table address just before its use.
    Four things got it from 93 to 25 and three are worth reading:
      - D_8009B20C is reached as `%gp_rel(D_8009B20C + 0x2)`, which is a
        member access on a SMALL-DATA object. An unsized array is never
        small data whatever -G says, so the declaration carries a size --
        `s16 D_8009B20C[4]`, eight bytes, still clearing -G8. Unsized costs
        a %hi/%lo pair at every touch and seven instructions of length.

        retail branches out on it and puts it last.
      - the counter's store has to be written inline in the compare's own
        statement -- `D_8009B20C[1] = *(u16 *)&D_8009B20C[1] + 1;` then a
        fresh read for the test. Through a named `m` the store is scheduled
        after the sign extension instead of before it (31 against 25).

    with the base, with the index, flat, and in a named offset -- 61 for the

    base locals are punished), naming the two loaded values above the first
    store (27) and below it (25, no change), moving the whole `e`
    computation above the +0x1A store (33) and the `m` read alone (32).
    Progression for anyone picking this up: 93, 40, 31, 25.

func_80031574 MATCHED 2026-08-30 by PORTING Unchiga's C (docs/MERGE_UNCHIGA.md);
  the history below is what this tree had reached on its own.
func_80031574_WAS -- 24 differences and ONE missing instruction, all of them in
    the tail block after the last call. Everything up to there matches: the
    ten-argument call, the shifted-and-masked switch selector, and the four
    cases with 0x14 and 0x17 sharing one body (two separate bodies with the
    same statement do NOT cross-jump here and cost two instructions; the
    shared `case 0x14: case 0x17:` spelling is what gives retail's three
    blocks plus a shared store).
    What is missing is retail's `addu $v0,$s3,$zero` -- a plain copy of the
    object pointer into a caller-saved register, with all ten tail stores
    then going through the copy. Writing `e = o;` before the block does not
    produce it: gcc coalesces the copy away and stores through $s3, which is
    correct code and one instruction short. This is the extra-copy-is-an-
    extra-name tell in the case where the obvious extra name is not enough.
    Measured and not it: the tail name in both orders, the four cases as
    separate bodies (+2 length), `case 0x17` written first, and the switch
    in plain ascending order.
    Three more spellings of the copy measured afterwards and all identical
    at 24: `e = (u8 *)(s32)o;`, `e = o + 0;` and `e = &o[0];`. gcc coalesces
    every source-level copy of a pointer into itself.
    UPDATE, 24 -> 14. The permuter found it and the mechanism is worth more
    than the number: a copy of a pointer into ITSELF coalesces away, but an
    assignment of a *derived* address does not -- `o = e + 8;` is a dead
    store (o is never read again) that gcc still allocates, and allocating
    it is what produces retail's `addu $v0,$s3,$zero`. Any non-zero offset
    works (4, 8, 0x18, 0x1A, 0x24, 0x44 all give 14); `o = e + 0` folds back
    to the plain copy and is 24 again. Where it goes matters as much: at the
    TOP of the tail block it is 14 with retail's copy in the right place, at
    the bottom -- which is where the permuter first put it, inside the last
    expression as `*(u16 *)(o = e + 8)` -- it is 16 with the copy emitted
    after all the stores.
    This is the dead-assignment register hint (func_800135FC) in its
    sharpest form yet: the assignment has to be of something gcc cannot
    prove equal to what it is copying.
    The permuter's own best-by-score output was a different candidate that
    retyped the fifth parameter to `unsigned long long` -- it measures 16
    but the caller (func_8003353C) stores exactly one word at 0x10($sp), so
    the parameter is 32 bits and that candidate is wrong. Reading the caller
    is what settled it.
    What is left at 14: retail does the +4 word's read-modify-write before
    the +8 halfword's and we do them the other way round.

func_8007214C -- 59 differences at +2 length, and the diagnosis is one

func_8005BE3C -- 75 differences at -6 length; the decode is settled and the
    open half is how many times D_8009B498 is reloaded.
    Retail loads that pointer global THREE times -- once for the x group,
    once for the y group and the func_8008FD38 base, once for the
    func_8008FDB4 base -- and we load it once and keep it. Neither writing
    the global inline at every reference nor reassigning a base local once
    per group produces the reloads: gcc CSEs all of them, so it is treating
    the `sh` through the derived pointer as not aliasing the pointer itself.
    Declaring the global `volatile` DOES produce them (95 built against 93,
    74 differences) but that is a knob with no justification -- nothing here
    needs it and no other file uses the symbol -- so it is NOT installed and
    the candidate carries the plain declaration. Whoever picks this up
    should look for the real reason gcc reloads before reaching for it.
    Settled, from the listing:

         D_8009B060)`; the scaling is `sll 3, subu` = 7k then `sll 13`, and
        the second argument is the `lbu` twenty instructions earlier, which
        is the early-evaluated-argument shape.

         *(s16 *)(...0x4242C) * *(s16 *)(...0x4242E) / 2)`; scaling is

func_80071FC8 -- 54 differences at equal length; gcc hoists two more loop

func_80039794 -- 43 differences, six of them real, and the decode plus three
    addressing decisions are done. Structure: four 0x64-byte records from
    D_800EB0F8 walked by two cursors (the record base for the three calls,
    base+0x30 for the flag word at +4 and the pointer at +0), an inner retry
    loop around func_800393B0 bounded by D_8009B0C1, and a tail that maps
    D_8009B35A through D_801D9000 into func_8003B50C's argument.
    Installed and load-bearing:
      - `-fno-strength-reduce`. Without it gcc rebases the +0x30 cursor at
        +0x34 (the flag field is read five times, the pointer twice) and
        every displacement in the loop is wrong. Sixth user.
      - D_8009B0C1 declared `u8 [8]` behind a per-file _IS_AGGREGATE guard,
        assembled at `-G4`. Recipe branch 3: it needs the non-small form
        while D_8009B35A (2 bytes) and D_8009B356 (1) stay gp-relative, and
        it is *narrower* than the widest of those, so no real threshold
        exists. With the plain unsized array gcc hoists its `%hi` out of the
        inner loop as an invariant, which retail re-materialises.
      - a base local for D_801D9000 assigned before the loop. Written
        against the symbol, `&D_801D9000[v] + 2` folds the +2 into the `%lo`
        and the hoisted invariant becomes base+2; against a register base
        the +2 stays a displacement, which is what retail has.
      - `k = -1;` unconditionally with `if (v >= 0)` overwriting it, the
        delay-slot form; `goto` rather than `else` for the 0x1C00 arm; the
        `for` third clause in the order `q += 0x64, j--, p += 0x64`.
    What is left, all in the preheader and the block that follows it:
      - gcc hoists the constant -1 (both `D_8009B35A = -1` stores) and puts
        it LAST among the preheader instructions; retail has it second, right
        after the loop counter. Every ordering of the three named setup
        assignments leaves it last, so the position is the loop pass's, not
        the source's. Naming it (`m1 = -1;`) does put it second -- and then
        gcc hoists the *store* out of the inner loop as an invariant and the
        whole `reset:` block disappears (-3). Naming it for one of the two
        sites only is +3. That coupling is the thing to break.
      - retail hoists the second `lhu 4($s1)` above the `sh $zero` to
        D_8009B35A and we do not, so we spend a `nop`. Writing the store
        after the test, or naming the read, are -4 and -1.
      - the first `D_8009B35A = -1` uses the loop counter's own -1 register
        in ours ($s0) and the hoisted one in retail ($s4); swapping the two
        statements changes nothing.
    Flag sweep on top of the installed pair: nothing better.
    Handed to the permuter.

func_8002A4A8_WAS -- 14 differences, two register rotations, and the other 96

    early if unchanged, then writes a cursor record at D_800EA1E8 --

    velocities `((s16)x - field) << 16` divided by the third parameter.

    is a variable -- here it is simply the parameter, no local needed.

    later-live local as a temp. `y = a;` in the `a < 10` arm with the modulo
    taken of `y` is 7 (a fresh local in the same place is 14), and

    second velocity's own variable, assigned for real much further down.

    over the subtract that feeds it, and ours writes $a0. One name for the

func_80025D30 -- 38 differences at -1 instruction; the addressing is solved
    and what is left is one constant's materialisation. Decode: a five-step
    timer that decrements D_8009B20C[1], reloads it to 0x10, picks a record
    out of D_800907D8 by `D_8009B1D0 + (D_8009B1D5 * 20 + 5)`, and if the
    record's 0x8000 flag is set spawns a func_8002C604(0xD) packet carrying
    the record's 0x30/0x32/0x34 halfwords, `D_8009B1D0 << 14` added into the
    packet's +0x14 word, and a 2/-0x1F4 or 1/-0x3E8 pair chosen on
    `D_8009B1D2 == 0x15D`; then bumps D_8009B1D0 and sets 0x40 in D_8009B220
    at five.
    Two things landed and both are now WORKFLOW lines or close to it:
      - the `-G4` window. D_8009B260 needs the bare form while D_8009B20C
        stays gp-relative, and D_8009B20C is eight bytes, so no threshold
        exists as declared. The unit touches only element [1], so a per-file
        `D_8009B20C_HALF` guard declares it `s16 [2]` and the window opens.
        Without this the `%hi` is hoisted into a branch delay slot retail
        leaves as a `nop`.
      - naming the 0x34 read and storing it after the +0x14 update. That is
        what puts the packet pointer in retail's $a1: the named halfword
        occupies $a0 across the branch, and every one of the ten sites that
        touch the packet was wrong before it.
    What is left: retail materialises the -0x1F4 / -0x3E8 that goes into the
    packet's +0x12 at the END of each arm, reusing the register the record
    update just freed, and we hoist it to the top of the arm into a register
    of its own. `s16 k` is the best of the four types (an `s32` k also makes
    the record's own subtraction an `addu` through that register instead of
    retail's immediate, which is +1); `u16` is the same as `s16`, and
    `0 - 0x1F4` changes nothing. Naming the shifted counter, in either
    position, is -2.


func_8004A940 -- 77 differences at -3, after m2c corrected the decode in
    three places the hand-read had wrong. THE SIGNATURE WAS WRONG: it takes
    FOUR parameters, not two, it RETURNS a value, and func_8004A8E4 takes
    four arguments. m2c saw it because $a2/$a3 are read before being written
    on the path where the first loop does not run, and because the `addu
    $v0,$a0,$zero` this entry used to call "a dead copy of the loop counter
    into a register nothing reads" is the return value being set to the loop
    index. Two of the three things this entry listed as open were that bug. Five loops, all over the same 0x28-byte
    records starting at D_8009B458 and bounded by `*(s16 *)(base + 0x510)`:
      1. bail out entirely if any record's 0x183 byte is 0x63;
      2. take the first record whose 0x18D byte is zero;
      3. take the first record matching (arg0 & 0xFF) at 0x183 AND
         (arg1 & 0xFF) at 0x185 -- this one reloads BOTH the base and the
         bound every iteration, where 1, 2 and 4 hoist them;
      4. loop 2 again;
      5. otherwise pick the record with the smallest unsigned halfword at
         0x19E, and give up if that is still 0xFFFF (`0xFFFE < best`
         unsigned).
    The tail calls func_8004A8E4(index, byte) where the byte is
    `arg0 & 0xFF` -- and on loop 3's exit gcc does not recompute it, because
    $a1 already holds the 0x183 byte the branch just proved equal to it.
    That is the shape to write: the loop-3 arm passes the loaded byte, the
    other arms pass the masked parameter, and the `jal` cross-jumps.
    Solved: the bound has to be written INLINE as
    `*(s16 *)(D_8009B458 + 0x510)` in loops 1, 2 and 4, not named. Retail
    tests the raw load in the guard and the loop compares against a hoisted
    copy of it, which is exactly what an invariant load in the condition
    gives; a named `n` gets one register for both and is -3 instructions.
    Also corrected: loop 1 compares the record byte against the VARIABLE
    holding 0x63 (retail keeps it in $a3 and does `beq $v0,$a3`), not against
    the literal; the literal costs a spare copy. 92 -> 80 on that alone, and
    80 -> 77 with the first loop's bound written inline.
    Still open, and it is the known reload class: retail re-reads the
    D_8009B458 pointer global at the bottom of loop 3 every iteration, and
    gcc hoists ours into a base local however the source spells it -- the
    same thing func_80071FC8's `hi` does, unsolved there too.

  **2026-08-28: the +1 was a `nop` in a load-delay slot, and the fix is a new
  declaration arm.**  Retail hoists the D_8009B0F4 reload ABOVE the
  `p + 0x1C` store and fills the reload's own delay slot with the `lui` for
  0x10000; gcc will not hoist a load of a non-volatile global past a store
  through `p`, so it emits the store first and the slot gets a `nop`.  A
  named read folds under the `_SIZED` (plain array) arm.  `D_8009B0F4_SIZED_

  named read AND lets gcc move it, because gcc 2.8 moves a volatile load
  across a non-volatile store.  101/101.

  it is about the *aggregate* arm, not about volatile as such.
