# --- guard sweep, 2026-08-20 -------------------------------------------------
tools_src/sweep_guards.py's first run, over every parked candidate at or below
50 differences. One match promoted (func_8003353C) and three parks improved:

  func_80013998   26 -> 20 at equal length, D_8009B118_SIZED
  func_8002C604   28 at -1 -> 24 at equal length, D_80010000_IS_AGGREGATE
  func_800496C4   38 at -6 -> 33 at -5, D_8009B458_IS_AGGREGATE

All three installed. A fourth hit is recorded and NOT installed:
func_8004A8E4 goes from 21 at -2 to 21 at -1 on D_8009B458_IS_VOLATILE. The
length error improves and the difference count does not, which is the weakest
shape a hit can have, and `volatile` on the game's main record array is a large
claim to make on it. Left for whoever next reads that function's residue.


func_8004D75C -- 40 differences at equal length (110/110), and every one of
    them is a register. The instruction sequence is identical: two loop nests
    over one 0xE20-byte record, the first walking a linked structure and
    accumulating byte weights, the second taking a running maximum per row.
    What paid, in order:
      - all three of the first nest's cursors are **givs**, not source
        variables: `t + i * 4` (retail's `addu $t2,$a3,$zero` + `lw 480($t2)`),
        `i * 2` and `i * 2 + 0x74`. Writing the first as an index and the other
        two as explicit scalars is what retail has -- an all-index version
        collapses the `+0x2C8` store into a t-based giv and loses 5
        instructions, an all-explicit one builds the wrong giv for the first.
      - the `v >= 0` arm is out of line, after the negative path's blocks, so
        it needs a `goto`, not an inline `if`.
      - `s = (u32)v >> 16; s = s & 0x7F;` as two statements against one name.
        Retail leaves the `bgez`'s delay slot empty because `s` is live on the
        taken path; one expression puts the shift in a dead temp and the
        filler hoists it. Now a WORKFLOW rule.
      - the second nest's index, cursor and counter need **their own names**.
        Reusing the first nest's is 63 differences and separate names are 40 --
        no instruction moves, the shared name just outlives its phase and
        outranks `t` in the allocator. Also now a WORKFLOW rule.
    What is left: `t` should be `$a3` and is `$t1`, `o` the reverse, and the
    hoisted 0xFFFF is materialised fifth in the preheader where retail has it
    second. Everything else follows from those.
    Measured and not installed, both with their exact shapes:
      - hoisting `b = r; u = n;` above the second nest's entry guard puts `t`
        in `$a3` and is 31 differences -- but at +1, because gcc then biases
        `r`'s giv by 0x7C4 and the two per-iteration copies retail has
        disappear. Ten shapes tried to keep the allocation and the structure
        together (index-based outer loop, explicit `b = t + n`, the zero store
        through four different lvalues, `for` versus `do`/`while`, and the
        dead-assignment hint, which gcc deletes because a plain copy
        coalesces); none does. `-fno-strength-reduce` is 85 -- it removes the
        givs the first nest needs.
      - the permuter reaches 21 differences by wrapping the second nest's
        store in `if (r || t) { store } else { store }`, identical arms that
        gcc cross-jumps back. That is semantically a no-op and correct, and it
        does put `t` in `$a3` -- but nobody writes it, and the
        `do { } while (0)` that stands in for this shape elsewhere does
        nothing here (40). Recorded rather than installed; a plausible source
        shape with the same effect is the open question.
      - declaration order does nothing: four orders, all 40.


func_8002FD10 -- 8 differences at equal length (112/112), and all eight are the
    *position* of one three-instruction group in the prologue. A screen
    set-up: it zeroes three globals, registers a callback with func_80014E1C,
    resets three 0x14-byte records, spins on func_80082324, then runs a 25-step
    loop that blits two sprites per step and walks a 5-wide grid.
    What paid:
      - the addressing. Two `$at` stores (D_8009B146, D_8009B148) beside
        gp-relative D_8009B2A0 (4 bytes) and D_8009B2A4 (2), so the window is
        `4 <= G < 8`: `D_8009B146_SIZED` declares both [4] and the unit
        assembles at `as -G4`. Same pair of knobs as func_8002E470.
      - `D_80010000` and `D_800EAE98` each want a size in the window
        `4 < N <= 8` -- at or below cc's own -G8 so cc1psx emits the *bare*
        symbol, above the assembler's -G4 so the assembler expands it. A bare
        symbol is one instruction to the scheduler, so the `lui`/`lo` pair
        stays in the destination register; the unsized array gives cc1psx's
        own splittable pair and it lands in a temp. `[0x40]` on D_800EAE98 --
        an honest size for a table that reaches +0x3C -- does NOT work,
        because it clears cc -G8 too. That is the sharp edge of this knob and
        it cost a round.
      - `lhu` not `lh` on D_800E9D70[5]: `*(u16 *)&D_800E9D70[5] + 1`, since
        the symbol is declared `s16 []`.
      - the three-record loop wants `p += 0x14;` before `i++`, not after.
    What is left: retail materialises `p = D_800EAE98` at prologue position 6,
    after the call's argument set-up and the `$s0` save; we put it at position
    1, before the `sh $a0` of the first global store. Nothing in the source
    moves it. Measured: four placements of the assignment (all 8), `p` written
    before the D_8009B2A4 store (8), `&D_800EAE98[0]` (8), naming the callback
    pointer (8), naming the two zero arguments (8) -- five shapes at the same
    number, which is the tell that this is not a source axis at all. Placing

    row is best; the permuter's only output is 22 and semantically broken.


func_80024E58 -- 2 differences at equal length (116/116), and both are the

  plus an explicit `if (n != 0) { do … while }` -- the guard reads through
  `p` and the body through `r`, which no `for` can express, and that is
  retail's `addu $a0,$a2,$zero` in the preheader.  An explicit byte offset
  `k` in the last loop for retail's `addiu $v1,$v1,2`.
  Measured and dead: base locals for 0x801E2800 and 0x801E4000 (much worse,
  -2 and 108 -- keep them as literals), four positions for the `i = 0`,
  four declaration orders, dropping `q` or `r`, `-fno-schedule-insns` with
  and without the strength-reduce flag, `-O1`, `-fno-function-cse`.  A
  permuter run of 125 iterations from this base found nothing.
  The two scheduling positions that are not the rotation: retail materialises
  0x801E4000 whole (`lui`/`ori` adjacent) before loading the pointer, where we
  leave the `ori` twenty instructions later filling an `lhu`'s delay slot; and
  retail keeps the 0x442 store where it is written, where we hoist it next to
  the 0x388 store because both consume the same 0xFFFF.  Retail's 0xFFFF
  therefore lives in `$a0` across the block, which is the extra long-lived
  value that pushes `p` to `$a2` -- so the two are the same problem.

func_80021598 -- 161 built against 158, and the residue is gcc *eliminating
  two induction variables that retail keeps*. The loop walks five cursors: a
  Rec20 record, that record + 1, two into D_801D5608, and the D_8009B1E8
  pointer carrying a running total at +0x2C. Retail strength-reduces exactly
  one of them -- the D_801D5608 pair, `$s2 = $s4 + 0x78` with negative

  0 to 0x17. We get biased givs instead: `$s0 = $a0 + 44` and `$s1 = $s3 + 6`,
  so every displacement in the body is shifted and the preheader stores move
  to a different base.
  `-fno-strength-reduce` removes the biases *and* the giv retail has, which is
  -4. Writing the second D_801D5608 cursor explicitly (`u = t + 0x1E;`) and
  taking the flag gets to -1 with 134 differences, but that source shape --
  a second cursor into the same array at a fixed +0x78 -- is not something
  anyone writes, so it is recorded as a measurement rather than installed.
  Measured and dead at default flags: moving `f += 0x20;` from the middle of
  the body to the end, which should move the bias from +5 to 0 by the
  bias-follows-the-last-use rule and moves nothing at all.
  The rest is right first try: one `v` for every loaded field (retail passes
  the loaded register straight to the call and stores the same register), the
  `lb`/`lh`/`lbu` widths per field, and the accumulate written as
  `*(s32 *)(p + 0x2C) = *(s32 *)(p + 0x2C) + func_80021558(n, v);` so the
  reload lands where retail has it.

  154, and what it changed is one expression: `v = (*(new_var = &f))[8];` --
  taking the address of the cursor, into a variable that is then dead. That is
  a **seventh** instance of the not-installable permuter class and a new
  sub-kind: not an uninitialised read this time but an address-taken local,
  which is precisely what stops gcc replacing the biv. Same lesson either way
  -- the mechanism is now named, and no plausible spelling of it has been
  found.

func_80013154 -- 131/131 with `-fno-strength-reduce` (recorded in build.py;
  without it gcc builds a biased giv for the +0x5150 store and the function is
  +5), and 117 differences, nearly all of them a register rotation downstream
  of two loop-invariant hoists retail does not have: the `2` stored at +0x5110
  and the loop bound `arg0 + 0xA2C0`. Retail materialises both *inside* the
  loop -- `addiu $v0,$zero,2` and `ori $v0,$zero,0xA2C0` / `addu $v0,$s4,$v0`
  every iteration -- and keeps five callee-saved values; we hoist both, need
  seven, and every register after them shifts.
  This is the fourth instance of the standing open problem (func_8004A518,
  func_80071700, func_80021598 are the others): gcc hoists small loop
  invariants where retail does not, and the permuter only reaches it through
  shapes that cannot be written.
  Probed directly on this one, since the length is right and the mechanism is
  visible. Three things that do NOT change it: adding named temps inside the
  loop (more pseudos), naming the `2` as a local, and `(s32)`/`(u32)` casts on
  the bound comparison -- all 117, unchanged. One thing that does: writing the
  bound as `p - arg0 < 0xA2C0` stops the *bound* being hoisted and takes the
  count to 79, but costs an instruction (+1) and leaves the `2` hoisted, so it
  is a measurement rather than a candidate. So the bound and the constant are
  hoisted by the same pass but respond to different things, and neither
  responds to pseudo pressure.
  The addressing is right and was the interesting part: SIX one-byte symbols
  (D_8009B142/3/4 and D_8009B14A/B/C) need the bare `lui $at` store while
  SEVEN one-byte symbols beside them (D_8009B0A0..D_8009B0D0) keep %gp_rel.
  Equal widths, so no real threshold -- the `_SIZED` arms declare two bytes and
  the unit assembles at `as -G1`, which is the same knob as D_8009B318 and
  the first time it has been needed for a whole group rather than one symbol.
  D_8009B0A3 needed a new `_IS_SCALAR` arm for the same reason in reverse.
  Also right: `six = 6;` as a local (retail's $s3 serves the D_8009B0A1 store
  and both +0x5124 / +0x514C stores), and the 20-byte unaligned copy at the
  end as a `struct { u8 b[20]; }` assignment.

func_80040DD8 -- MATCHED 2026-08-21. Kept here only for the two levers it
  established, both of which apply elsewhere:
  * Name a GROUP of loads before the group of stores that consumes them. gcc
    cannot hoist a load through one `u8 *` above a store through another, so
    retail's batched loads have to exist in the source. Two sites here took it
    from 170/164 and 121 differences to 164/164 and 22.
  * **One name for two sequential constants.** The last 22 were the eight
    `- D_8009B146` / `- D_8009B148` subtractions: retail loads each offset with
    `lh` into the SAME register `$a2`, one after the other. Two named locals
    `dx`/`dy` gets the `lh` (that alone is invisible in the count -- still 22 --
    and is the reason ten earlier spellings all read as "wrong axis"); ONE name
    reused for both, assigned again between the two groups, is 22 -> 8. The
    residue was then the four X statements written in the target's *store*
    order rather than its *load* order; writing them 8 / 0x10 / 0x18 / 0x20 is
    a MATCH.
  Its sibling func_80041068 (12-byte stride, 182 instructions) MATCHED the
  same afternoon by adapting this source field-for-field -- the two levers
  above, `as -G0` (it has no `%gp_rel` at all), and one declaration:
  `D_800EFE42` needs to be its own symbol, because `D_800EFE40[1]` relocates
  as `%lo(D_800EFE40+2)`, which is the same address under a different name and
  reads as two differences. That was the whole residue.
  The third sibling, func_8004158C, carries the same block at 472 instructions
  and is still open.

Permuter re-score sweep, 2026-08-21 (score_permuter_outputs.py over the whole
park). Six stored outputs beat what was installed; every one was read before
being trusted, and the split was three not-installable, one decomposed to a
real one-line lever, and two open.
  * func_800434F4 32 (from 35) -- DECOMPOSED and installed: the only change is
    `*(s32 *)(p + 0x1C) = 0x800;` moved between the two D_801AF000 stores.
    It transfers to func_80020BE4 and func_8003B808, and it is the seed of the
    whole run of improvements to that family recorded above.
  * func_80032184 27 -> 22 -- NOT INSTALLABLE, and worth reading anyway. The
    change is `new_var = 1;` in arm m0 and `p[0x46] = new_var;` in arm m2, so
    m2 reached without m0 reads an uninitialised local. The plausible spelling
    is already available -- this function has `s32 one; one = 1;` -- and
    `p[0x46] = one;` is **27, no change**, so the gain is specifically a pseudo
    that is *defined in the other arm* rather than one that is merely equal to
    1. Fifth instance of the borrowed-name class and the first where the
    honest version is this close to hand and still does not fire.
  * func_8002C6C8 58 -> 25 -- NOT INSTALLABLE: it moves `q[0] = q[0] | 0x40;`
    to after the `return`, i.e. deletes a store the function makes. Its other
    half is legitimate and was measured -- hoisting `r = D_800F2848;` out of
    the loop -- but the candidate is -1 instructions and the hoist makes it
    +2, so the two counts are not comparable and the hoist is not a win. The
    -1 is the thing to chase in that function, not the permuter's number.
  * func_80045208 25 -> 17 -- NOT INSTALLABLE: `if (e) { A } else { A }` with
    two identical arms round the switch's `default`. The `do { … } while (0);`
    that stood in for the same shape on func_8005F3B8 does **not** reach it
    here -- both placements are 25. So the duplicate-arm idiom and the
    do/while(0) idiom are not interchangeable; whatever gcc does with two
    identical arms it does not do with one loop node.
  * func_80046768 46 -> 5 and func_80021598 154 -> 27 -- both already recorded
    above as the uninitialised-read and address-taken-local classes.
  * func_8003B744 18 -> 8 -- already recorded as semantically wrong (it casts
    an index the target does not mask).

  a one-at-a-time hand sweep cannot find and the permuter can. Read a

  * `v = 9;` -- borrowing the *dead* local that held `*(s32 *)(e + 4)` for the

  negative.**  270 substitutions tried, **96 failed to compile**, 0 better --
  and the tool's own warning fired, which is what that failure counter is for.
  The failures are its documented limitation: on the outermost block, which
  spans the declarations, renaming `a` to `b` leaves two declarations of `b`.
  With thirteen locals this function is almost all outer-block pairs.  Do not

func_8002E128 -- 8 differences, first written 2026-08-21. 146 instructions,
    MATCHED 2026-08-31 by PORTING Unchiga's C from his tangos/work branch (docs/MERGE_UNCHIGA.md). It compiled to the retail bytes under cc1psx 2.8.1 unchanged -- the park below records what OUR source shape could not reach, and is kept for that.
  gp=0 at=0, default flags, no addressing work at all.
  A card-artwork setup routine: decodes a BCD card id
  (`((n >> 4) & 0xF) * 10 + (n & 0xF)`) into an index, then reads a per-card
  flag byte from one of two tables -- D_80090C00 with six-byte entries for ids
  >= 0x200 and D_80090BA8 with two-byte entries below -- and calls
  func_8002E060 for each bit set. Both symbols were added to variables.h.
  Three things were needed and all three are existing rules:
  * `s32 arg1`, not `s16`. Retail has `addu $s1,$a1,$zero` with no sign
    extension, so the `< 0` test is on the raw word; the narrow declaration
    adds `sll`/`sra` and is +1 and 144 differences.
  * **the else branch's flag byte needs its own name.** Reusing `n` for
    `o[0]` there -- which looks right, because retail reuses `$s1` for it --
    is 49 differences, and a separate `m` is 22. Every one of those 49 is a
    register, and the split fixes the *whole function's* allocation, not just
    that block. Note this is the opposite of what the listing's register reuse
    suggests, which is the standing warning about reading register reuse as
    name reuse.
  * `p[0x10] = 1;` written *above* the two read-modify-writes on the object
    rather than below them (22 -> 19), and a base local for D_80090BA8 in the
    else branch (19 -> 18).
  * **the else branch's index wants its own name AND an `s16` declaration**,
    and the second half is the real lever. The sequence is worth writing out,
    because the first reading of it was wrong:
      - inline in the address expression: 18
      - a fresh `s32 k`: 19 (worse)
      - borrowing `q`, the mutually exclusive arm's `s32` local: 12
      - a fresh **`s16 k`**: 8
    So the borrow was a partial proxy for the narrowing, not the mechanism.
    The permuter reached 9 by declaring the borrowed `q` itself `short`, which
    is **not installable** -- `q` also holds `*(s32 *)(p + 0x28)` in the other
    arm and a 16-bit truncation of that is a wrong address -- and the two
    plausible spellings that reproduce it, a fresh `s16 k` or `s16 q` with a
    separate `s32 r` for the pointer, are both 8, i.e. better than what it
    found. Read a permuter win for its *mechanism*: here the semantically
    illegal candidate pointed straight at a legal one that is better.
    The `u16 *` pointer local and the `&=` it also carried are worth nothing.
    Where the `* 2` sits also matters: inside `q` is 12, moved to the
    indexing (`&b[q * 2]`) is 19, because retail materialises the table base
    *before* the scaling.
  The residue is 8 in one cluster, and it is two instructions' *placement*
  rather than any instruction being wrong: the RMW block itself is
  instruction-for-instruction right, but retail materialises the `1` for
  `p[0x10]` between the `sw 4` and the `sh 8` and does not store it until four
  instructions later, after the *6 index scaling, while we emit the `addiu`
  and the `sb` together at whichever end the source puts them. Thirteen
  placements and spellings were measured -- the statement at five positions
  among its neighbours, `one` as a local at three positions, the RMWs as
  compound assignments, a `do { } while (0);` round the pair, and both reads
  named -- and they are 11, 15, 18, 19, 21, 22, 23 or 30 -- the four placement
  probes were re-run against the 8 base after the `s16` landed, in case the
  levers were coupled, and they are not. Permuter rerunning from the 8 base.

func_8004BE88 -- MATCHED 2026-08-21. Kept for the one lever that is new and
  general:
  **A switch that retail compiles to a comparison tree and you compile to a
  jump table wants a CASE RANGE sharing the default label.** gcc 2.8 takes the
  table when `count >= 4` and `range <= 10 * count`; five cases over
  0x2F..0x59 is 42 against 50, so it took it. Retail's tree opens `bltz $a1` /
  `slti $a1,0x10`, which are the lower-bound tests for a case node at 0
  spanning to 0xF -- so the source is `case 0 ... 0xF:` immediately above
  `default:`, the switch's range is 89, and gcc builds the tree. Semantically
  a no-op; 127 differences to 86 and the dispatcher byte-for-byte right. The
  function is a MIDI meta-event dispatcher and 0x00..0x0F is the text-event
  range, so the original wrote it to document that.
  The rest was existing rules: the divide split into two statements against
  one name (86 -> 57, and it also stops gcc sinking the D_8009B458 load and
  store past the `divu`), the scalar arm of D_8009B458 plus `as -G0`
  (57 -> 48, the func_8002CEE8 delay-slot lever), two names for its two reads
  (48 -> 44), an explicit guard plus `do`/`while` with `off += 0x2C;` before
  `i++` (44 -> 6), and `off = 0; i = 0;` in that order (6 -> 2).
  The last 2 came from the permuter and decompose to one line: `z = 0;` at the
  top of the arm, with `off = z; i = z;`. Retail zeroes one register and
  copies it into the other, and a literal 0 in both places gives two
  independent zeroings whichever order they are written in. Twelve spellings
  had been measured against that residue first, so this is the shape to reach
  for when two initialisations to the same constant come out as two
  materialisations and the target has one.

func_80071924 -- +12 instructions and 125 differences, first written
    MATCHED 2026-08-31 by PORTING Unchiga's C from his tangos/work branch (docs/MERGE_UNCHIGA.md). It compiled to the retail bytes under cc1psx 2.8.1 unchanged -- the park below records what OUR source shape could not reach, and is kept for that.
  2026-08-21. 144 instructions, gp=0 at=0, default flags. Parked on a single
  mechanism and recorded mainly for that.
  The whole gap is **gcc's loop-invariant motion hoisting three comparison
  constants that retail recomputes inside the loop**. The body tests a
  loop-invariant `b` against 1, 4, 5 and against the range [3,5]; retail
  materialises `addiu $v0,$zero,4` and `addiu $v0,$zero,5` twice each, in
  branch delay slots, and computes `addiu $v0,$s3,-3` / `sltiu $v0,$v0,3`
  inside the loop. We hoist 4 and 5 into `$a2` and `$fp` and the range check
  into a stack slot -- five preheader instructions -- and the two callee-saved
  registers they take then spill two other values, which is the other half of
  the +12.
  Nine source spellings and eight flag rows were measured against it and
  **every one is +12 or worse**: the `&&` chain as nested `if`s, the `||`
  chain as separate `if`s with a shared label, the range check as
  `(u32)(b-3) < 3` / `b >= 3 && b <= 5` / `b == 3 || b == 4 || b == 5` / a
  named intermediate, one name for the table base and the record cursor,
  `-fno-cse-follow-jumps`, `-fno-cse-skip-blocks`, `-fno-rerun-cse-after-loop`
  and `-fno-expensive-optimizations` (all four exactly +12/125, which is the
  usual tell that CSE is not the mechanism). `-fno-strength-reduce` is the
  only row that moves it, to +8, and a `switch (b) { case 1: case 4: case 5:
  }` for the first test is also +8 but builds a different comparison tree.
  What IS right and worth keeping: `sllv $v0,$s1,$s6` in the preheader is
  **gcc's own** constant-1 register for the `* 12` index scaling, not a source
  local -- writing `one = 1;` and using it for the three `== 1` comparisons
  scores identically to using literal 1s everywhere, so the `one` reading is
  unnecessary. The three-way tail (`a == 0` / `a == 1` / else compare the two
  fields) is instruction-for-instruction right, and so is the loop's
  `do`/`while` with the address-taken `sp14` re-read from the stack each
  iteration.

func_80048A28 -- 65 differences at the right length (121/121), first written

  `w2 = (u16)(arg2 + 0x80);` hoisted to the top of the `if (t != 0)` block

  test and not the pair. Every instruction is in the right place; the whole residue is
  one register rotation across the function, which is why the count is so
  high against so little wrong.
  What is right: `D_8009B45C` as the **scalar** arm plus `as -G0` (gp=0, so

  gcc hoists the `%hi` out of the loop into a callee-saved register and every
  one of the five reads becomes a `lw` off it; the bare form is one
  pseudo-instruction, nothing to hoist, and retail's `lui`/`lw` per use comes

func_80058624 -- 133/133, ONE difference, first written 2026-08-21.
  A panning/attenuation setter: two `func_80086E50` square roots over the pair
  of 12-byte vectors at D_800F56F0, a distance-derived volume clamped to
  [0x10, 0xFF], and a signed pan clamped to [-0x7F, 0x7F].
  gp=0, at=0, and sweep_try says every row from `-G0` to `as -G4` gives the
  same 1 -- so the addressing is not the axis and default flags are installed.
  Five things decided it, and four are recorded levers firing exactly as
  written:
  * **The two locals are one `u8 st[2]` whose address is taken.** Retail
    stores zero to both slots before the first call and then overwrites both
    a few instructions later; gcc deletes the first pair as dead unless the
    object is address-taken, and it also materialises `addiu $s4,$sp,0x11`
    into a *callee-saved* register. `s8 *pp = (s8 *)&st[1];` assigned **above
    the first call** gives both -- assigned later it folds into the store's
    own addressing and lands in $a2, which is -3 and a whole register class
    wrong. This is the address-taken-local rule (WORKFLOW step 4) used
    deliberately rather than diagnosed as a problem.
  * **The `- 0x2BC` on the first square root belongs after the SECOND call**
    -- func_8005A6A8's lever, second instance. Written next to the call gcc
    fuses it into `addiu $s0,$v0,-700`; moved across the second
    func_80086E50 the raw result has to survive in a callee-saved register
    and retail's `addu $s0,$v0,$zero` / `addiu $s0,$s0,-700` pair appears.
    That one move was -1/86 to 133/133 and 16.
  * **The quotient reuses the name of the distance** (`r`), whose live range
    ended at the `r > 0` test -- the mutually-exclusive borrow. A fresh `q`
    puts it in $a0 where retail has $s0. 16 -> 5 with the next one.
  * **`st[1]` is read `lb` at the calls and `lbu` would be wrong**: the array
    is `u8` because element 0 is, so the pan read needs the lvalue cast
    `*(s8 *)&st[1]`, not `(s8)st[1]`. 8 -> 3.
  * The clamp chain is **three assignments and two names, not one**:
    `w = r / 16; v = w; if (w < 0) v = -v;` … `w = v; if (r <= 0) w = -w;`
    -- the third assignment reusing `w` is what puts the second copy back in
    retail's $v0. One name throughout is 10; `w`/`v`/`u` as three names is 3.
  Residue: retail negates `$v1` in place (`subu $v1,$zero,$v1`) and we negate
  the copy's source (`subu $v1,$zero,$v0`). Same layout, same delay slot, same
  registers otherwise -- gcc has folded one copy through. Ten spellings
  measured, all exactly 1: testing `v` instead of `w`, `0 - v`, `v -= v + v`,

  the pair and round the `if` alone, a redundant `v = v;`, and a trailing
  `w = v;`. Six borrowed names for `v` were also tried (`dx` 26, `dz` 20,
  `a` 3, `b` 3, `c` 16, `ex` 7) -- none is better than the plain name.
  This is a permuter case: one instruction, one operand.

func_80038EB0 -- 164/164, **29** differences (147 when first written; three
  permuter reads and one hand lever took it to 37, 36, 34 and 29 over two
  days). First written 2026-08-21. 164
  instructions, a byte-stream command dispatcher: it reads two bytes from a
  cursor selected by `p[0x58]`, looks the command up in the three-entry
  D_800EB010 table (Rec4C, 0x4C bytes each) and either updates a slot or
  creates one. gp=4, at=0, one `%hi`, so no addressing work.
  **The count is inflated and says almost nothing** -- the FIRST difference is
  the prologue, because we save a sixth callee-saved register that retail does
  not, and every instruction after it is shifted by one. Read the first
  difference here, not the 147.
  What is right, and was worth finding:
  * **The two byte reads are `p[0x58]` read twice, not a cached cursor.** The
    `*c = e + 1` store may alias `p[0x58]`, so gcc reloads -- writing it twice
    is what the target does and it comes for free.
  * **The first byte needs two names.** Retail has `lbu $a1,0($v0)` … later
    `addu $s3,$a1,$zero` -- the caller-saved-to-callee-saved copy at the
    definition, func_800386B8's tell. `a = e[0]; … cmd = a;` produces it; a
    single name does not. Worth 15 differences. The *second* byte's copy comes
    for free and splitting it explicitly changes nothing.
  * **The second read must NOT have a `u8 **c` local.** With `c` the pair
    `lui`/`addiu %lo(D_800EB010)` is emitted after the address computation and
    the `lb`'s load-delay slot takes a `nop`; written inline
    (`e = *(u8 **)(p + p[0x58] * 4); … *(u8 **)(p + p[0x58] * 4) = e + 1;`)
    the scheduler straddles the pair across the `lb` exactly as retail does.
    That was +1 to 0 on the length -- and it is the whole reason the earlier
    134-at-+1 reading looked better than the correct 149-at-0. **Do not
    compare counts across length errors**; this function is the cleanest
    instance of that trap in the file.
  * The `>= 0x41` / `< 0x41` split, the `goto have;` join after the three-way
    table lookup, and `goto create;` out of the `fl & 0x20` arm are all
    retail's own layout.
  **The sixth saved register is gone, and the lever is worth knowing.** gcc
  was sharing `cmd >= 0x41` between the `create` block and the test AFTER
  `func_80039F44`, so the comparison result lived across the call and took a
  callee-saved register -- the sixth, which shifted every `sw`/`lw` in the
  prologue and inflated the count to 147. Retail recomputes `slti $v0,$s3,0x41`
  fresh after the call. **No spelling of the same comparison breaks that:**
  `cmd > 0x40`, `!(cmd < 0x41)` and `0x40 < cmd` are all canonicalised back and
  score identically. What breaks it is an ARITHMETIC on `cmd` before the
  compare -- `cmd + 1 >= 0x42`, `(cmd + 1) > 0x41` and `cmd - 1 >= 0x40` all
  give 147 -> 37, because the compared value is then a different expression and
  there is nothing to share. The permuter found `(cmd + 1) > 0x41`; all three
  spellings are equally contrived and equally correct, so the installed one is
  a judgement call and the MECHANISM is what to remember: when a comparison is
  shared across a call and the target recomputes it, add and subtract one.
  Splitting the second byte into two names is then worth one more (37 -> 36);
  on the 147 base it was worth nothing, which is the "arms that score worse in
  isolation" rule again.
  Two more after that, both small and both worth recording for their kind:
  * **`b = cmd + 1;` as a NAMED local, borrowing the second byte's dead
    name** -- 36 -> 34. A FRESH name for the same expression is worth nothing
    (36). Third instance in three days of the same shape: the permuter's win
    is a borrow, and the fresh-name control proves it is the allocation and
    not the naming (func_8005BFC8's `t2` and its `u16 *`, now this).
  * **`do { } while (0);` round the single `*(s16 *)(q + 0x40) = v;` store**
    -- 34 -> 29. gcc sinks that store below the two constant stores that
    follow it and retail emits it first; the loop node pins it. Round the
    whole `v = 0x178; if …; store` block scores the same, so it is the store
    that needs pinning.
  Residue at 29: register naming in the two byte-read blocks, one `addu`
  operand order on read 2 (base-first in retail, index-first in ours -- and
  read ONE comes out base-first from the same spelling, which is the puzzle),
  and the `create` block now sharing the `cmd + 1` form. Measured and
  worthless on the 29 base: `&p[...]`, the negation trick and a named scaled
  index for read 2's address; `cmd - 1`, `cmd + cmd` and `-cmd` for the
  post-call test (34, 34, 45); `cmd | 0` folds straight back to 147.
  The old residue, for the record: Measured and worthless: `cmd > 0x40` and
  `!(cmd < 0x41)` at either site or both, an `if`/`else` for `k` (148), five
  placements of `q = (u8 *)D_800EB010;`, three names for the reused `v`
  (147 -- the func_800154E4 rule fires but only just), and four compiler flag
  rows including `-fno-schedule-insns`. This is the register-allocation class:
  run the permuter on it.

func_800580D4 -- 123 target, 120 built (-3), 44 differences, written
  2026-08-21. A light/matrix packet builder: it picks a 0x50-byte record,
  builds two MATRIXes on the stack, copies one to the other, and fills the
  caller's out-parameter with three `0x1000 - x` angles.
  **The stack layout came out exact on the first draft**, which is the part
  worth keeping: gcc 2.8 allocates locals at increasing offsets in
  DECLARATION order starting after the 16-byte outgoing-args area, so writing
  them in the order the listing's `sp+NN` constants appear reproduces the
  frame with no iteration at all. Here that is pkt(0x10) sv60(0x60) m68(0x68)
  sv88(0x88) sv90(0x90) m98(0x98) svB8(0xB8), and every offset in the built
  code matched immediately.
  It also needed a new type. `include/types.h` now has `Matrix` -- 3x3 `s16`,
  two bytes of pad, three `s32` -- because the 32-byte by-value copy expands
  as eight aligned `lw`/`sw` only at alignment 4, and that expansion is the
  tell (WORKFLOW's Rec1C rule read forwards). The 8-byte copy at the end is
  `SVector` and expands `lwl`/`lwr`, which is alignment 2 and confirms the
  pair of types rather than one.
  What else is right: `y = sv60.vy;` named once and used for both the
  `pkt.m0.t[1]` store and func_8008E3D0's fourth argument -- retail reads that
  halfword ONCE and we read it twice, 93 differences to 50 on that line -- and
  writing D_800F2C40 inline rather than through a base local (50 to 44).
  Residue, and the two halves look coupled: retail carries arg2 in $s3,
  D_800F2C40 in $s2 and arg3 in $s1 -- which is exactly usage-frequency order,
  most-used in the lowest register -- and we have that rotation inverted. And
  retail RELOADS `*(s32 *)(D_800F2C40 + 0xD18)` three times where we load it
  once and keep it in $v1, which is the -3. The reloads are most likely
  register pressure rather than aliasing: get the rotation right and they may
  follow. Measured and worthless: `volatile` on any subset of the three reads
  (it moves in steps of TWO, a load plus a `nop`, and never lands on the +3
  needed), the byte-address cast, a local copy of arg2, a base local used only
  for the 0xD18 reads, and `*(s32 *)arg3 = 0;` hoisted to the top (-4).

func_800580D4 -- the blocker retraction that preceded the draft above: the standing notes said it "needs PsyQ MATRIX/SVECTOR types". It
  does not. The listing has **zero** GTE/COP2 instructions (`wc2|rtps|mfc2|
  mtc2|cop2|lwc2|swc2` all match nothing), gp=2, at=0, and all seven calls go
  above 0x80073840 into the PsyQ library, where an implicit declaration is
  enough. `SVector` already exists in include/types.h. The real difficulty is
  the stack frame: ~0xB8 bytes of locals that m2c renders with overlapping
  offsets (sp10/sp14/sp28..sp5C), i.e. arrays it could not size, plus a
  pointer to a stack local stored into the out-parameter at the end. It is the
  smallest open candidate (123) and it is open, not blocked.


func_8002BD0C -- 140 target, 140 built, 19 differences, written 2026-08-21,
  reworked 2026-08-28 from -1/113.  The seventh member of the D_8009B0F4
  dispatcher family and the first with a real jump table. gp=0, at=5,
  `as -G0`, and the default (volatile scalar) arm of D_8009B0F4 for the
  back-to-back read-modify-writes in arms 0 and 2.
  **The dispatch and the jump table came out exact on the first draft**, which
  is the hard part for this class: seven cases whose arms sit in memory in the
  order 0, 1, 2, 4, {3,5}, 6 -- read straight off `jtbl_80010208` in
  `asm/data/800.rodata.s`, since the table's `.word`s give the case-to-arm map
  and the arm addresses give the layout. Written in that order with
  `case 3: case 5:` sharing, plus two `goto` join labels (arm 0 jumps to the
  one arm 4 falls into; arm 1 jumps to the one arms 3/5 fall into), it
  reproduces retail's block layout exactly.
  Three levers closed the -1 and took 113 to 51, and **all three were already
  in the file as measured negatives**, because each is worth nothing until the
  other two are in:
  - **arm 2's `d` had to be a second name.**  Arm 2 and the join1 arm both end
    `sw 8; addiu 0x800; sw 0xC`, so with one name they get one pseudo, one
    register, and the final jump pass cross-jumps arm 2's tail into join1 --
    two instructions short.  Retail keeps arm 2's pointer in `$v1` and
    join1's in `$v0`, and that register difference is the ONLY thing blocking
    the merge.  A second name is +2 and restores the length.
  - **`do { n = 0x20000; } while (0);` in arm 0.**  Without it gcc fills arm
    0's `j` delay slot by duplicating join1's `addiu $v0,0x800` and hoists the
    `lui` for 0x20000 up into the middle of the arm; retail has the `lui` in
    the slot.  -1 to 0, and 117 to 66.
  - **a named read of D_8009B0F4 above the `+6` store in arm 0** (66 -> 56)
    **and a SECOND, differently named one in arm 2** (56 -> 51).  The old
    entry recorded the arm-0 read alone as "+6 worse", which it is; it only
    pays once the length is right.  One name shared by both arms is 56, so
    this is the func_8003B808 rule ("do not share the name across two switch
    arms") a second time.
  Then the one-name-per-arm lever from the sibling func_80057544 took 51 to
  32, in three steps and with one surprise in each:
  - **arm 4's `0x100` is not arm 2's.**  Both arms build the same four-
    halfword packet, so one `hun` reads as obvious; retail holds arm 2's in
    `$s0` (it survives the call and is stored again afterwards) and arm 4's in
    `$v1` (dead at the call).  One name forces the callee-saved register on
    both.  51 -> 41, and giving arm 4 its own `g` as well is worth nothing.
  - **`n` wants one name per JOIN GROUP, not per arm.**  Arms 0 and 4 reach
    join1 and arms 1, 3, 5 reach join2, so the value really does cross arms --
    `n2` for the join2 group is 41 -> 35 and a third name for arm 2's own
    `n` is 41 -> 38, but the two together are 38, not 32.  They interfere.
  - **`d` wants splitting at the same boundary** (a third name for the join2
    group): 35 -> 32 with the join-group split, and 32 either way, so the
    installed candidate carries the two-name `n` and the two-name `d` and
    leaves arm 2 alone.
  What is left is 32 register differences with the instructions
  instruction-for-instruction right: retail holds the mask `m` in `$a0` and
  `n` in `$v1`, and we have `n` in `$a0` pushing `m` to `$a1`.  All six
  declaration orders of m/n/d score identically, which is the usual tell that
  the axis is elsewhere.  745 permuter iterations from the 32 base found
  nothing better than 32.  From the earlier 51 base it reached 47 by writing
  join2's two stores as `d = (*(s32 *)(p + 8) = D_8009B118);`, which emits
  them in the opposite order to retail's; not installed.

  **2026-08-28 evening, 32 -> 19**, on three edits from the two siblings that
  matched the same hour:
  - **one name per distinct mask VALUE** (the sibling rule): 32 -> 27.
  - **arm 4's two constants are ONE name.** Retail holds 0x26810 in `$v1`,
    stores it to `p+0x30`, then puts 0xA000 in the same `$v1`.  Written as a
    literal store plus a separate `n = 0xA000;` the two get different
    registers; `n = 0x26810; *(s32 *)(p + 0x30) = n; n = 0xA000;` is 27 -> 24.
    This is func_80020BE4's one-name-for-two-sequential-constants rule, third
    instance.
  - **arm 2's `n` is not the join groups' `n`.** It is the only 0x1C value
    that reaches no join label, and a third name for it is 24 -> 19.  Measured

func_8004803C -- 157 target, 157 built, 140 differences, written 2026-08-28

  A sound-voice setup: it looks a 16-bit id up in one table, derives two
  volumes, applies an optional pan, and writes eleven fields of the
  D_8009B45C block before two library calls.

  SHIFT and not a divide (the `/ 128` further down does have the `bgez`/
  `addiu 0x7F` bias and is a real divide); and `sllv $v0,$s1,$v0` with `$s1`
  holding 1 across two shifts is the `one = 1;` named-constant tell, the same
  shape as `and` versus `andi` for a mask.
  arg5 and arg6 are `u8` on the callee's own evidence -- their stack slots are
  read with `lbu`.
  **The -4 was three things and none of them was the `andi` lead the first
  entry named.**  Retail's `andi $a1,$a3,0xFF` is reproduced by the obvious
  `v = arg3 & 0xFF;` as soon as `v` stops being reused for the pan block's
  products -- that reuse was the whole of it, and the "we multiply $a3 raw"
  reading was wrong.  The other two: every index sum is written **index
  first** (`n + *(s32 *)(b + 0x444)`, not the reverse), and `idx` is a
  **`s16`**, not an `s32` -- with `s32` gcc knows the `lhu` already bounds the
  value and folds the second `& 0xFFFF` away, so the `andi` retail re-emits
  at the tail block has nowhere to come from.  Declared `s16`, gcc keeps the
  raw halfword and re-masks.  157/157 with that.

  arg5/arg6 in $s3/$s0/$s5/$s4, the id in $s2 and D_8009B45C in $a2 (with
  `addu $t2,$a2,$zero` saving arg2 out of the way), and we hold D_8009B45C in
  $t0 and need no such copy.  The count is inflated: the diff is positional
  and the prologue's store order differs, so almost every later line is
  marked.
  Measured and worse: splitting the base local per block (-1/153, and the
  three-name split is what *causes* the duplicate `lhu` to disappear, so the
  two interact), splitting `n` into two names (-1/157), naming arg2 (141), and

  "one name or many" -- it is which values the target keeps in one register
  across the arms, and the answer here differs per variable within one
  function.

func_80046294 -- 151/151, 5 differences, written 2026-08-28.  A queue
  compaction pass over the D_8009B45C block: it walks 0x30-byte records from

  at +0x4C, and then does a small amount of flag work on +0x7C and +0x40.
  gp=0, at=0, `as -G0` -- retail reads D_8009B45C through a `%hi`/`%lo` pair
  at every one of its eleven sites and the default -G8 makes it gp-relative,
  which is -10 instructions on its own and was the whole of the first draft's
  length error.
  **Both jump tables were recovered from `asm/data/800.rodata.s` before a line
  of C was written**, which is the cheap part of this class:
  jtbl_800106C0 is base 0x20 with 41 entries -- index 0 to the `case 0x20`
  arm, 34/35/37/38/40 (0x42 0x43 0x45 0x46 0x48) to the copy arm, the rest to
  default -- and jtbl_80010768 is base 0x42 with 7.  Six written cases over a
  range of 41 clears gcc's `count >= 4 && range <= 10 * count`, so the first
  switch really is a table and not a tree.
  Three levers, in the order they paid:
  - **the loop is `while (1)` with an `if (…) goto after;` head test and two
    `break`s at the bottom.**  A `do { … } while (i < n && i >= 0);` with the
    same head test inside is -10 and then +2: gcc rotates the head test to the
    bottom and merges it with the loop-back, so the head's `lh`/`beq` pair
    disappears.  Writing the head test with a `goto` label instead keeps it
    but loses the loop entirely -- the jump-table base stops being hoisted
    into the preheader (+1).  `while (1)` keeps both: 151/151 and 13.
  - **`(bp + off)[0x80]` rather than `bp[off + 0x80]`** for the command byte,
    which is the documented `addu base,index` versus `addu index,base` (13 to
    12).
  - **`do { i = 0; } while (0);` written ABOVE the entry guard** -- 12 to 6 for
    the pin (it moves `i` from `$t0` to retail's `$a3` and `boff` the other
    way) and 6 to 5 for hoisting it above the `if`.  All six declaration
    orders of i/off/boff score identically, which is the usual tell that the
    axis is the assignment and not the declaration.
  What is left is 4 instructions of preheader order -- retail emits the
  hoisted jump-table base BEFORE `boff = 0x30; off = i;` and gcc emits it
  after -- plus one `%lo(.rodata+168)` against `%lo(.rodata)`, which is the
  SECOND jump table's offset inside splat's single rodata blob and may well
  be a try_func artefact: only the full build can prove a jump table landed.
  Measured and worthless on the preheader: a `do`/`while` round both
  statements (12), round `boff` alone (12), and swapping their order (5, no
  change).

  scalings feeding func_80049F10, and func_80044DC0 at the end.
  gp=0, at=0, `as -G0` -- **thirteen `%hi`/`%lo` reads of D_8009B45C**, which
  the default -G8 turns into single gp-relative loads.  That alone is -13 to
  -4 and 159 differences to 64, and it is the same first move as
  func_80046294, func_800164FC and func_8002BAB4.
  Each clamp step reloads the symbol twice, which the inline spelling gives
  for free: gcc has no global CSE and a store through the pointer kills the
  cached load.
  The -4 is all in the SECOND scaling: retail keeps the `if`/`else` as two
  arms with a `j` to a join and then sign-extends the joined value with
  `sll 16`/`sra 16` before the call, and gcc collapses our `if`/`else` into
  an unconditional `b = 0;` plus a conditional overwrite -- the func_80026BA4
  shape -- and then folds the `(s16)` away because after `andi 0xFFFF` and
  `srl 8` it can prove the value is 0..255.  The FIRST scaling has the same
  shape and comes out exactly right, so the difference is the cast at the
  call.  Measured and worthless: a named `s16` local for the call argument,
  a `do { } while (0);` round the `else`, and writing it as
  `b = 0; if (a) b = …;`.
  `func_80049F10` has no prototype in functions.h and `src/func_80049F10.c`
  matches with `(s32, s32)`, so the sign-extension is the source's own cast
  rather than a conversion.

  **2026-08-29, -4 to -1 on one declaration: `s16 b`, not `s32 b` with an
  `(s16)` cast at the call.**  The cast folds -- after `andi 0xFFFF` and
  `srl 8` gcc can prove the value is 0..255 -- and the narrow declaration
  makes the truncation part of the assignment instead, which restores three
  of the four missing instructions (the `j` to the join, the `else` arm's

  The last -1 is the sign-extension itself: retail does `sll $a0,$v0,16` /
  `sra $a0,$a0,16` at the join and we copy the already-narrow `b` into `$a0`
  with one `addu`.  So retail's `b` is an SImode value extended AT the call
  and ours is a 16-bit value extended at the assignment; no spelling found so
  far gives the first without folding the extension away.

  **2026-08-29, -1/63 -> 169/169 and 9, from the permuter: the second clamp's
  upper test assigns its LOAD into `b` inside the condition.**
  `if ((b = *(s16 *)(D_8009B45C + 0x1580)) >= D_8009B45C[0x1584])` -- `b` is
  the call argument three blocks later and is dead here, so this is the
  assign-to-a-not-yet-live-local hint (func_8002A4A8's class), but spelled as
  an ordinary C idiom rather than a bare no-op.  It is the whole -1 AND
  fifty-four differences.
  **The exact spelling matters**: `b = (x >= y);` and
  `if ((b = x >= y) != 0)` -- assigning the COMPARISON rather than the load --
  are both 45.  The permuter's parenthesisation is the thing to read, and a

func_80023D08 -- 173/173, 69 differences, written
  2026-08-29.  A card-cursor mover: `arg1` is a direction bitmask (bit 0

  Three things the first draft got right and are worth reusing:
  * `func_8002348C()` is called with NO argument register set at all, and
    `src/func_8002348C.c` takes `u8 *arg0`.  That is the original's missing
    prototype: `$a0` still holds this function's own `arg0` at that point, so
    an implicit declaration emits nothing.  Do not add a prototype.
  * both `div`s are by the variable `n` (`break 7` in the listing), and the
    second arm's SHIFT is `sllv` by the same `n` -- so that arm is
    `(x << n) / n` with `n` a variable, while the first arm is `(x << 8) / n`
    with a literal.  gcc 2.8 has no global constant propagation, so `n = 8`
    assigned before the call does not reach the use after it.

    into `%lo(D_80090800+2)`; a base local (`tb = D_80090800;` assigned right
    after the call) is what keeps retail's `%lo(D_80090800)` with `2($v1)` on

  value into the record and immediately reads it back signed.  gcc forwards
  the store and emits `sll 16`/`sra 16` on the register instead, which is one
  instruction fewer and cascades.  `*(volatile u16 *)(p + 0x2C) = …` for that

  only thing that does.  Measured and worthless: a volatile READ (+2), a

  a struct-view type on the store, and the whole 24-row flag sweep.  A
  genuinely aliasing store between the two -- `D_8009B162 = 0x40;` moved up,
  or a dummy byte store -- also brings the reload back, which is what
  identifies the mechanism as cse's memory invalidation; but both put the
  global store in the wrong place.  The volatile is installed as a
  placeholder and the plausible spelling is still open.
  What is left is 69 register differences in two clusters, both in the first
  arm: the five-argument `func_80022D94` setup (retail computes

2026-08-29, guard sweep, and BOTH hits are worth writing down as method.
  `sweep_guards.py` over 205 parked candidates and 84 guarded symbols
  returned two rows, and one of them is a **false positive of exactly the
  kind the score-ranking caution describes**.  func_8003BF00 goes from
  111/112 and 35 to 112/112 and 55 under `D_8009B118_SIZED` -- the LENGTH
  comes right, which by the usual ordering makes it the better base.  It is
  not: the guard changes `extern s32 D_8009B118` into `extern s32
  D_8009B118[2]`, so every `b = D_8009B118;` in that file silently becomes
  the symbol's ADDRESS rather than its value, and the listing plainly has
  `lw $v0,%lo(D_8009B118)($v0)`.  The instruction that "came back" is the
  `addiu %lo` of an address computation the function is not supposed to do.
  So: **a `_SIZED` or `_IS_AGGREGATE` hit on a symbol the candidate reads as
  a bare scalar is an array-decay bug, not a lever** -- check every
  unsubscripted use before believing the count.  func_80030D5C's hit is
  genuine (it already writes `D_8009B0F4[0]`), and is 29 -> 28 at -1 on
  `D_8009B0F4_SIZED_VOLATILE`; installed.

  **2026-08-29, 17 -> 2, and the whole drop is ONE embedded assignment plus
  one un-split store.**  The permuter's output was
  `v = (*(s32 *)(p + 0xDF0) = *(s32 *)(p + 0xDE0) + sum);` -- the DF0 store's
  own value becomes `v`, which is func_8002FB78's chained-assignment idiom
  and is ordinary C.  17 -> 5.  Then the DF4 store wants the addition INLINE,
  `*(s32 *)(p + 0xDF4) = v + *(u16 *)(p + 0xE02) * 4;`, not `v += …;` on a
  line of its own: with the chained assignment above it, `v +=` allocates the
  sum a second register and lets the store sink into the `bne`'s delay slot.
  5 -> 2.  Measured and worth 5 (i.e. nothing): a name for the multiply,
  `v = v + …`, naming only the halfword, and a `do { } while (0);` round the
  store.  Measured and worth 9 from the 17 base but subsumed:
  `v = *(s32 *)(p + 0xDE0); v = v + sum;` split against one name.
  The last 2 are the preheader order: retail has `addu $a1,$a2,$zero`
  (`i = sum`) before `ori $t1,0xFFFF` and we have them the other way round.
  Nine spellings measured, all 2 or 3: every ordering of the three preheader

    splits it -- func_800300C8's rule again.

    `lwl`/`lwr` for bytes 0..3 and `lh`/`sh` for 4..5, which is exactly

  This is the sweep the WORKFLOW says to run after any batch of permuter

  * **func_80021598: +3 and 154 -> 158/158 and 27**, on ONE statement:
    `fp = &f;` where `fp` is a `u8 **` that is never read again.  Taking the

    fixes the LENGTH too.  Position does not matter (at the top of the

    `f[0]` everywhere is +15 and 163, and `u8 * volatile f` is +20 and 167 --
    both put it in memory and both are much worse.  So the lever is the
    address being TAKEN, not the storage class, and no plausible spelling has
    been found.  What is left is one bias: retail keeps `$s0` = `p` and
    writes `44($s0)`, we hoist `p + 0x2C` into `$s0` and write `0($s0)`.
    Four spellings of that accumulator (`((s32 *)p)[0xB]`, an `(s32)` cast,
    the constant written first, and the whole 24-row flag sweep) are all 27.
  * **func_8002BD0C: 19 -> 14**, on `if (d2) { A } else { A }` round the
    join1 arm's two stores -- semantically a no-op that gcc merges back, and
    the gain is that `d2` (the OTHER arm's name for the same global) is READ
    there, which extends its live range past the branch.  Plausible spellings
    that do NOT reach it: `do { … } while (0);` round the same two stores
    (-3 and 68), `d2 = d;` before them with the stores through `d2` (-1 and
    65), the same with the stores through `d` (19), and `d2 = d;` after them
    (19).  Installed as a placeholder; the spelling is open.
  * **func_80020988: 72 -> 46**, which was first written up here as "does
    NOT decompose" and does: the whole 46 is `do { } while (0);` round the
    tail's `D_8009B152 |= 0x40` (the named `0x2000` is worth nothing and the
    identical-arms `if` is the same lever spelled by the permuter).  From
    that base the function MATCHED on 2026-09-03 -- see the commit -- and
    the `do`/`while` ended up round the OTHER read-modify-write.

  **The full run: 65 candidates checked, 18 with a better stored output, and
  one of them was a MATCH nobody had opened.**  func_8005D994 was parked at 5
  and its `output-0-1` directory held a zero score.  Decomposed, only ONE of
  its three edits is load-bearing:
  `first = *(s8 *)&D_8009B07A; x = first;` -- borrowing the name of a local
  whose own live range starts further down, which is the func_8002A4A8 class.
  The other two (`0x80 | arg0` instead of `arg0 | 0x80`, and `a[2] +=`
  instead of `a[2] = a[2] +`) are worth nothing alone and are NOT installed.
  MATCHED 2026-08-29.
  The remaining fifteen are unread and are the seam to work next; each is
  `build/permuter/<f>/output-*-1/source.c` re-scored against the installed
  park, and each still has to be decomposed before it goes in:
    func_8002C6C8  58 -> 25      func_80047480  60 ->  9
    func_80037DA4  61 -> 54      func_8004803C 140 -> 98

2026-08-29, working the score_permuter_outputs list.  Five taken, one MATCH.
  * **func_80047480 MATCHED**, and the permuter's win decomposes to ONE
    moved store: `*(s32 *)(p + 0x438) = 0x1010;` written after the
    `p + 0x43C` store instead of before the `p + 0x388` one, which is 60
    differences to 9.  The named zero it came paired with is worth nothing.
    The last 9 were the counter-init lever twice over: `i = 0;` hoisted out
    of the outer `for` clause AND `j = 0;` hoisted out of the inner one,
    with `k = i << 6;` between them.  `i` alone is 6, `j` alone is 3, both
    is the match -- so this is a coupled pair of the same lever, and doing
    one and stopping would have read as a near-miss.
  * **func_80048D08: -1 and 59 -> 131/131 and 19**, on `d = D_8009B45C;
    p = d;` -- borrowing the name of a local whose own assignment is two
    lines further down, the func_8002A4A8 class.  It fixes the length too.
    The `do { } while (0);` it came paired with is worth nothing.  What is
    left is `j` and `k` exchanged in `$t3`/`$t4` plus one copy's position;
    measured and all 19: both counter-init hoists, the declaration order of
    `j` and `k`, and reading `+0x444` through `d` rather than `p`.
  * **func_8004D75C: 40 -> 21** on `if (r || t) { A } else { A }` round one
    store -- the same implausible identical-arms hint as func_8002BD0C, and
    again `do { … } while (0);` round the same store is worth NOTHING (40).
    Reading `r` and `t` at that point is the mechanism.  Installed as a
    placeholder.
  * **func_8004A518: +1 and 73 -> 112/112 and 27 on `u16 o2`** -- and this
    one is a FALSE ZERO worth spelling out.  The +1 in the `s32` version is
    gcc hoisting `addiu $s6,$zero,99` (the `e2[3] = 0x63` store's constant)
    out of the loop where retail materialises it per iteration.  The narrow
    declaration does not fix that; it adds an `andi $v1,$s2,0xFFFF` retail
    does NOT have and separately suppresses the hoist, and the two cancel.
    `u8 o2` scores the same and is semantically WRONG (`o2` starts at 0x180).
    Installed at `u16` because the equal length makes the diff readable, but
    the `andi` is a known lie -- the honest problem is the 0x63 hoist, and a
    named constant assigned inside the loop, `for (o2 = 0x180; …)`, and
    moving `o2 = 0x180` above `i = 0` are all +1 and 73.

2026-08-29, the rest of the score_permuter_outputs list.  Six worked, no
match, and one finding that is worth more than any of them.
  **`if (x) { A } else { A }` is a LIVE-RANGE hint and nothing else, and
  there is no plausible spelling of it.**  Four instances now -- func_8002BD0C
  (19 -> 14 on `if (d2)`), func_8004D75C (40 -> 21 on `if (r || t)`),
  func_80045208 (25 -> 17 on `if (e)`), func_80044608 (38 -> 33 on
  `if (D_8009AF7C)`).  In every one, `do { … } while (0);` round the SAME
  statement is worth exactly nothing.  The mechanism was pinned down on
  func_80045208, the smallest of them: a plain dead read (`p8 = e;`, `q = e;`,
  at either position) is also worth nothing, because gcc deletes it before
  allocation.  A BRANCH on `e` cannot be deleted -- cross-jumping the two
  identical arms happens after register allocation -- so the branch is the
  only thing that survives long enough to make `e` live at that point.  That
  is why the idiom works and why every legitimate spelling of "read this
  value here" fails.  All four are installed as placeholders.
  * **func_8004D58C: 13 -> 12** on moving `ff = 0xFFFF;` one line down, below
    `p3 = (u8 *)0;`.  The `new_var` for `t + 0xDF0` and the `!= 0` dropped
    from the test are both worth nothing.  What is left is the master
    register of five zero copies (`$t3` in retail, `$t5` in ours); twenty of
    the 120 orderings of those six initialisations were swept and none beats
    12.
  * **func_80037DA4: 61 -> 54** on a SECOND NAME for the opcode --
    `op2 = op;` assigned right after the read, used only by the
    `(op2 & 0x80)` test.  Using it for the `switch (op2 & 0xF)` as well
    scores the same, and using it ONLY for the switch is worth nothing, so
    the 0x80 test is the site that matters.
  * **func_80048A28: 65 -> 60**, two edits that compose: a name for
    `(arg0 & 0x1F) * 2` (65 -> 64) and writing D_8009B45C INLINE at the two
    sites in the `arg2 - 1 < 0x80` block instead of through the `b` local
    (61 alone, 60 together).
  * **func_8004803C: 140 -> 137**, and its 98 is REJECTED for length.  The
    two `arg1 & 0xFF` masks the permuter added are semantically free -- the
    function already does `arg1 = arg1 & 0xFF;` before them -- and they are
    worth 3 on their own at equal length.  Reaching 98 needs the
    identical-arms hint on top, and HERE that hint costs an instruction (+1),
    unlike the four cases above.  `u8 arg1` in the signature, with or without
    the explicit mask statement, is -1 and 154, so the byte parameter is not
    the reading either.

2026-08-31, func_80058624 and func_8005A6A8: ONE residue, TWO functions, and
the diagnosis that was in this file was pointing at the wrong thing.

Both end with the same block -- `w = h / 16;` then an abs, a clamp to 0x7F,
then a conditional negate -- and both leave exactly the same difference:
retail `negu $v1,$v1` against our `subu $v1,$zero,$v0`. Two independently
written functions with an identical residue is this file's own standing
signal that the cause is systematic rather than local pressure.

msearch settles what it is NOT. 110 spellings of the block on func_8005A6A8
(five abs forms x two clamp forms x eleven tails, including `goto`, both
polarities, the ternary, an in-place `v = -v`, and a dead `v = v;`) collapse
to ten distinct instruction streams and none beats one difference. The same
block on func_80058624: ten tail spellings, twelve `v`/`w` type combinations,
all 75 single-declaration widths. Plus the ten hand shapes and 411 permuter
iterations already recorded. **The tail's spelling is not the axis.**

What it IS, from reading the listing instead of the diff. func_8005A6A8 has
TWO of these sites and **the first one already matches**:

        sra   $v0,$v0,4          w = h / 16
        bgez  $v0,.L8005A82C
         addu $v1,$v0,$zero      <- the copy, IN THE DELAY SLOT
        negu  $v1,$v1            <- negate in place
    .L8005A82C:
        ...clamp...
        bgtz  $s0,.L8005A848
         addu $v0,$v1,$zero      <- same shape again
        negu  $v0,$v0

The two differ in one respect: the first branch tests the value being copied
(`bgez` on `w`), so the copy has to survive; the second tests an unrelated
variable (`bgtz` on `h`), so gcc coalesces `w = v` away and negates straight
out of `v`'s register. Retail keeps the copy anyway and puts it in the
branch's DELAY SLOT.

So the question is not how to spell the negate. It is what fills that delay
slot -- gcc has something else to put there and retail did not. That is a
different search, and the two functions will fall together when it is found.

  improved 2026-09-04. The debug/test
  menu step: it tears the menu down and rebuilds it on D_8009B23A's 0x4000
  bit, then steps a four-entry mode on the pad bits.
  Addressing: D_8009B394, D_8009B398 and D_8009B3A4 want the bare %hi/%lo
  form while D_8009B180/D_8009B184 (four bytes) stay gp-relative, so the
  window is `4 <= G < 8` -- the SIZED_VOLATILE arms plus `as -G4`. The
  volatile matters: the non-volatile eight-byte arms (a new `_SIZED` arm was
  added to variables.h for the probe) are -5 to -7 instructions, because
  retail re-reads each of those halfwords per path.

  `>= 2`, not switches** -- retail's tree tests 1, then `slti 2` for the
  default, then 2 and 3, which is four tests for three cases. The `switch`
  was measured: gcc balances three nodes and pivots at 2, and a fourth case
  would put the range inside the jump-table rule instead, so neither shape
  reaches retail's tree. One switch spelling measured, one reasoned about; the arms are `goto` targets in retail's address order;
  the dispatched byte is read into an `s32` local so the bound test is
  `slti` and not `sltiu`; the flag word is NOT a local (`f = D_8009B23A &

    in the D_8009B398 block -- and the second is a redundant copy. Deleting

  slot loader: it clears, restores or installs one of a record's slots and

  53 with the s32; `(p + i / 8)[0xBEC]` for the bit table so the `addu` comes
  out base-first; and `q++` before `i++` in both loops, which is what puts
  the cursor bump in the back edge's delay slot and the counter's before the
  bound load (-1 per loop).

  fills the `lbu p[0xE0F]` load-delay slot with `sll i*2` and leaves the
  `beq c, 0xFFFF` slot empty, where we put `n = 3` in the compare's slot and
  a nop after the load. The shift counts follow from it -- retail's `srav`
  and `sllv` take the count from n's own register, ours are `sra`/`sll` by
  the immediate 3.

  named quotient, a hand-written bias with `if` and with a ternary, and

  negation subtraction, constant-first, base-first), six declaration orders,
  and assigning `i = 0` above the entry guard (+1 each).
  And the whole binary was scanned for the idiom: `srav` with its count in a
  saved register holding a small constant appears in exactly ONE in-scope
  listing, this one. The four matched functions that use `srav` all shift by
  a genuinely variable count (func_8002CCA8's `0x80 >> (arg0 & 7)`), so the
  source shape behind this one is not something this tree has seen, and the
  permuter is the right tool rather than more spellings.

  either order with 0x12 first is 77. So the installed order is the optimum

    the read-modify-write folds, and the mechanism is one step removed from
    the usual rule: it is the POINTER load that becomes unforwardable, and
    once gcc has two independent pointer loads it can no longer prove the
    two field accesses touch the same object either, so the value fold goes
    with it. -11 -> -5. It must be unsized: at [2] it is eight bytes, small

    volatile -- gcc coalesces it -- and REQUIRED with it, because each RMW

The remaining two clusters:

  a) 2 differences -- the `|= 2` block. Retail materialises the base with
     `lui $v1 / addiu $v1,$v1` (one register, the local-alloc combine above);
     we get `lui $v0 / addiu $v1,$v0`. Giving THAT block its own name too is
     the obvious next step and is +1 and 97 -- it fixes this cluster and costs
     more elsewhere. Writing it inline is +1 and 97; `|= 2` rather than
     `= ... | 2` is identical.
  b) 8 differences -- the abs block. Retail's `subu` reads the COPY ($v0);
     ours reads arg3 directly, because gcc copy-propagates through
     `t = arg3`. The `do { } while (0);` round the copy does not stop it, and
     `if (t < 0)` and `if (arg3 < 0)` score identically, which is the
     wrong-axis tell. The ternary -- `arg3 = (arg3 < 0 ? -arg3 : arg3) * 2;`,

     entirely and is -2 and 82. The `ori 65534` / `lhu 4($s2)` order and the
     `sh 40($a2)` sinking into the `beq` slot are downstream of this.

Worth recording because it nearly went in as a false negative: an earlier test
of "separate names for the else arms" used ONE name shared by both arms, which
is still two blocks and therefore still global -- it scored 93, unchanged, and
read as "per-arm names measured dead". They are not; one name PER ARM is the
lever, and it was worth 8 of the 18.

register NAMES differ, and they differ because of one extra callee-saved
register.

Five things that had to be right first, all read off the listing:

  * **the head is an EXACT POINTER DIVISION.** `subu / sll1 / addu / sll4 /
    addu / sll8 / addu / sll16 / addu / negu / sra 3` builds v * 0x33333333,
    negates it (= v * 0xCCCCCCCD, the modular inverse of 5) and shifts by 3 --
    an exact divide by 40. That sequence appears ONLY for a typed pointer
    difference; `(arg0 - D_800F5788) / 0x28` on `u8 *` gives a mult/mfhi
    reciprocal and reads as a different function. It needs a 40-byte record
    type declared purely for the subtraction;
  * **the pointer difference is RECOMPUTED per block**, not held. Retail
    rebuilds that whole chain inside the `arg3 != 0` block. A local `i` is
    -11 instructions;
  * **the divisor is read per block too.** `lbu %gp_rel(D_8009B078)` appears
    TWICE. A local `d` holds it in a callee-saved register: 277 -> 226;
  * **the frame.** Retail saves SIX registers ($s0-$s4, $ra) at 0x60-0x77.
    `sw $s1, 0x10($sp)` is NOT a local -- it is the FIFTH OUTGOING ARGUMENT of
    func_8005FBC4, which takes six, so the outgoing area is 0x18 and the
    locals are 0x18-0x5F: three 4-pointer arrays and a 24-byte object, not 32.
    `0x88($sp)` is above the frame and is the fifth INCOMING argument, which
    confirms the five-argument signature independently of the caller;
  * **arg3 is s32, not u16.** As u16 gcc emits an `andi $s1,$s1,0xFFFF` in the
    loop preheader that retail does not have. That removal is what makes the
    length read as exact -- it cancels the giv's extra instruction, so the
    zero must not be trusted.

The one remaining fault: I emit THREE cursors in the final loop --
`addiu $s2,$sp,0x4E`, `addiu $s3,8`, `addiu $s4,2` -- where retail has TWO,
`addiu $s1,8` and `addiu $s3,2`, reading 0/2/4/6($s1) off a single cursor.
The extra is a biased giv for the `*(u16 *)(r + 6)` read, and it is the
seventh callee-saved register, which is why the frame is 128 where retail is
120.

Measured and dead on that fault:
  * `-fno-strength-reduce`, the documented lever for exactly this shape
    (func_800722CC): -1 and 270 on the corrected base, -1 and 252 on the
    earlier one. It removes givs retail keeps;
  * naming the +6 read and taking it before the `r += 8` bump, which is what
    m2c prints: identical, 226;
  * `m++` written inline before that read, dropping the `for`'s third clause
    (func_80061008's lever): -7.

Not yet tried: indexing sp48 by the loop counter rather than a cursor, a
separate counter name for the final loop, sweep_try, and the permuter -- which
is started on it, since "instruction-for-instruction right, only the
allocation wrong" is exactly its class.

One tooling note worth keeping: `grep -c 'break 7'` returns ZERO on this
function, which has seven of them. splat writes `break      7` with padding.
Use `grep -cE 'break +7'`. Same class as the `lui  *\$at, %hi(` trap.

func_8005EBF4 -- improved 275 -> 226 differences (still 287/287) on
2026-09-04. The permuter found it and it is ordinary C: the three
`b[i] = <expr>; ... a[i] = b[i];` pairs are CHAINED ASSIGNMENTS,
`b[i] = a[i] = <expr>;`. Same lever as func_8002FB78 -- a store whose value
the next statement also uses. The permuter chained only [0] and [1]; chaining
all three measures identically and is what a person would write, so that is
what is installed. The remaining fault is unchanged: one biased giv for the
`*(u16 *)(r + 6)` read, costing the seventh callee-saved register.

func_800528AC -- 288/288 and 125 differences, `-fno-strength-reduce` (now in
    build.py's PER_FUNC_FLAGS). Written from scratch on 2026-09-04; +55-
    equivalent to exact length in one session.
    Decode: a 10-iteration loop over 0x18-byte records at D_800F2B50. For a
    live record it takes a side from bit 1 of the flags word, indexes
    D_800F2C40 + side * 0xE20, saves that record's 4-byte field at +0xDC0
    into a stack Word4, writes an interpolated colour there, runs two loops
    over e[0xE1B] calling func_8004DC38, and restores the saved Word4. The
    colour is a real lerp with a RUNTIME divide (seven `break 7`, so the
    divisor is the variable `hi`):
        col.unkK = p[0x10+K] * (hi - lo) / hi + p[0x14+K] * lo / hi;
    for K = 0, 1, 2, with `col.unk3 = p[0x13];` written FIRST -- the listing
    stores 0x1B before 0x18/0x19/0x1A.
    THE FRAME WAS RIGHT ON THE FIRST DRAFT: `Word4 save; Word4 col; s32 i;
    u16 sv; s32 off;` lands at sp+0x10, 0x18, 0x20, 0x24, 0x28, exactly
    retail's. Note the 0x10 -> 0x18 step -- gcc rounds each stack local up,
    so two adjacent Word4s are eight apart, not four. That looks like a
    missing local and is not one.
    Levers that are IN the candidate:
      - **`hi`, `lo` and `sh` are `u16` locals.** Retail has three
        `andi 0xFFFF` that exist only when a u16 local crosses into int
        context; an explicit `& 0xFFFF` at a call site is NOT the same
        instruction. 233 -> 141, the largest single lever here.
      - `-fno-strength-reduce`, which removes a biased giv stepping alongside
        the cursor. On the pre-u16 base it read as a regression and it is not:
        with the u16 locals in, it takes the candidate from +3 to exact
        length. func_800722CC's trigger verbatim.
      - `u32 fl` for the flags word. It changes NOTHING in the difference
        count (249 either way) and it is still right: the census says the
        two `sra` become `srl` and all four shifts then match retail. A
        count-only comparison discards this edit; the opcode census keeps it.
      - `s32 v`, not `u8 v`, for the two inner-loop locals -- retail has no
        `andi 0xFF` there.
      - the tail written with NO local: `*(u16 *)(p + 0xC) = *(u16 *)(p + 0xC)
        + func_80058E1C();` then `if ((u32)*(u16 *)(p + 0xC) >= ...)`. The
        second READ is what stops the `sh` sinking into the branch delay slot
        (func_8003B378). The named-local spelling scored BETTER (239 vs 251)
        while the u16 fault was still open.
      - `D_8009AF9C = (s32)(D_800F2B50 + off + 1)`. The relocation is
        `%hi(D_800F2B50 + 0x1)`, literally +1; the first draft had +0x18 from
        misreading m2c's `&(D_800F2B50 + 1)[sp28]`.
    WHAT IS LEFT is three missing `addu` and three extra `nop`, and they are
    the SAME three: retail's copies fill load-delay slots that we leave
    empty. Adding any one copy in source costs +1 without removing a `nop`,
    so this is coupled allocation and not a naming question.
      1. the base copy. Retail emits `lui $v0,%hi / addiu $v0,$v0,%lo /
         addu $s5,$v0,$zero`; we emit the pair straight into the cursor's
         register, and every register name downstream follows from that.
         MEASURED AND DEAD, all at 288/288 and 249-250: `p = D_800F2B50 +
         off;` with off just set to 0; assigning p after i and off; a dead
         derived `e = p + 8;` beside it; `bp = D_800F2B50; p = bp + 0;`; a
         plain `bp = D_800F2B50; p = bp;` (coalesces); and
         `do { p = ...; } while (0);` (+2). func_8007308C's recipe needs a
         pre-loop use that dies before the loop and there is none here.
      2. `addu $a3,$s3,$zero` -- retail masks `sh` ONCE into a register
         before the first inner loop and copies it at the call; we emit the
         `andi` at the call site. An `s32 shv = sh;` before the loop is +1
         and 268.
      3. `addu $a3,$s2,$zero` -- retail holds `sv` in a register across the
         second loop; we reload `lhu 36($sp)` every iteration. An
         `s32 sv2 = sv;` before that loop is +1 and 272, and both together
         are +2 and 276.
    Flags measured on the final base, all worse or not comparable:
    -fno-schedule-insns 259, -fno-schedule-insns2 +6, -fno-function-cse 249
    (identical), -mno-split-addresses +2 and 166 -- and that last one is NOT
    a win, it is a different length AND structurally wrong, since retail has
    cc1psx's split pair here.
    Handed to the permuter.

func_800279BC -- 271/271, 14 differences, empty opcode census. Default flags,
    gp=4 at=0, no jump table. Candidate in parked/func_800279BC.c. This one
    went 161 -> 14 in one session on five source-shape levers and nothing
    else, so the entry is mostly a record of WHICH levers, because every one
    of them was rejected on an earlier base before it was accepted on a
    later one.
    INSTALLED, in the order they were found:
      1. Block layout as `goto` targets in retail's own address order
         (257 -> 199), and an `s32` return type with five `return 0;`
         (199 -> 190).
      2. In the `func_80026D18(c,0xA,0x14)` arm, write the `[9]` modulo
         FIRST and the two zero stores after it. Retail materialises ONE
         `%hi(D_800EAE88)` pair and lets the zero stores fill the `mult`
         latency; with the zero stores written first we emit a SECOND pair
         and the census carries `lui +1`. 161 -> 143, and the census went
         from three opcodes to one.
      3. ONE NAME for the index and the byte it reads: `r = n - 1;
         if (r != 0) r = func_800358FC(n); r = *(s8 *)(a[r] + 0x18);`.
         Retail carries both in `$a3`. 143 -> 128, exact length, census
         EMPTY. Twelve spellings of a two-name version -- borrowing `r`,
         `n`, `j`, a fresh `m`, a phase split, the value hoisted above the
         `n == 0` guard -- are all BYTE-IDENTICAL, so the axis is the
         merge, not the placement.
      4. `pb++` written AFTER the call, not before it. Retail fills the
         `bne`'s delay slot with `addiu $s0,$s0,4` and leaves the `jal`'s
         slot a `nop`; with `pb++` first, gcc sinks it into the `jal` slot
         and the delay-slot filler then takes the `hit:` block's first
         instruction (`lui $a3,26214`, the reciprocal) into the `bne` slot
         instead. 180 -> 67. `*(++pb)` and `pb = pb + 1;` are identical.
      5. The two `% 5` divide chains in the `check:` arm INTERLEAVE only if
         the second read is named and assigned BEFORE the `[0xA]` store
         (func_80026DC8's lever). In the `plain:` arm the whole modulo
         expression goes at the top of the block instead -- naming just the
         read there is -2 and 124, because the name narrows to QImode.
         Same idiom, opposite spellings, two arms of one function.
      6. `D_800EAE88[0xB] = 0;` moved AFTER `[0xA]` in the `check:` arm
         (30) and after `[0xA]` in the `hit:` arm (14).
    THE RE-MEASURE RULE PAID FOR ITSELF FOUR TIMES HERE, and that is the
    thing worth carrying away. Lever 6's `check:` half was measured on the
    z2 base as 185 against 180 -- WORSE -- and on the gg base as 30 against
    36. Lever 3's borrow spellings were byte-identical on the p1 base and
    the merge only became visible once lever 2 closed. An intermediate
    271/271 with an EMPTY census (y1, 128) was a FALSE zero: the scan
    loop's missing `nop` was cancelled by the un-interleaved chains, and
    fixing the chains made the length go to -1 before it came back. So a
    clean census is not a clean function while two faults can cancel.
    REMAINING, all pure register allocation:
      1. `i` is in `$a0` where retail has `$a2`, at all four of its sites,
         and the `$a0`/`$a1`/`$a2` rotation through the second half follows
         from it (10 of the 14). MEASURED AND DEAD at 271/271 and 14:
         `i = 0xA;` hoisted above the outer `if`, above the function's
         first store, `do { i = 0xA; } while (0);` on either assignment or
         both, and six declaration orders. Every one is byte-identical,
         which is the wrong-axis tell.
      2. `addiu $a1,$a1,-4` -- retail reuses one register for the
         sign-extend and the bias; we get `$a3`. `r = (s8)r; r -= 4;` and
         `r = (s8)r; r = r - 4;` are both +1 difference (15).
      3. `lbu $v0,d_8009b1d5` before `addiu $a1,$sp,16` -- retail
         materialises the 0x90000000 mask's `lui` before the read and does
         `pa = a` after. Moving `pa = a;` below the `h = ...` line is
         neutral alone (14) and NEGATIVE combined with lever 2's spelling
         (41), so the two are coupled.
    Handed to the permuter.

func_800528AC, follow-up 2026-09-04 -- 249 to 125 on a COUPLED PAIR the
    permuter found, and the half that looked like the lever was a passenger.
    Its stored output-3180-1 carried three edits. Two decompose to nothing:
    a `new_var = lo;` copy (249, no change) and swapping `j++; q += 4;` to
    `q += 4; j++;` in the second inner loop (-1 and 274, i.e. WORSE alone).
    The third was `if (side || p) { sd = X; } else { sd = X; }` around the
    side computation -- identical arms, and `side` is not assigned until
    seven lines LATER, so on the first iteration of the do-loop it reads an
    uninitialised local. That is WORKFLOW's first reject shape and it is not
    installable.
    IT DID NOT NEED TO BE. `do { sd = ...; } while (0);` in the same place
    is +1 and 153, and `if (p != 0) { sd = X; } else { sd = X; }` -- an
    always-true test on a local that IS initialised -- scores IDENTICALLY at
    +1 and 153. So the uninitialised read was never the mechanism; the block
    boundary was, and two legitimate spellings reach it. Then the
    `q += 4; j++;` swap, worth -1 on its own, closes the +1: together they
    are 288/288 and 125. Neither alone is anything, which is why a
    one-at-a-time sweep had missed both.
    The general lesson, and it narrows WORKFLOW's fourth permuter class:
    when a win depends on an uninitialised read, ask whether the read is
    load-bearing or whether the SHAPE around it is. Substitute an always-
    true test on an initialised value; if the score is unchanged, the read
    was a passenger and `do { } while (0);` will do.
    MEASURED AND DEAD on the new base: nothing yet -- the residue has not
    been re-read since the drop, and every entry in the old "measured and
    dead" list above was compiled against the 249 base and must be re-tried.
