func_8005AE68, follow-up 2026-09-04 -- 22 to 21 and the census from four
    opcodes to two, on ONE declaration. `lim` was `s32` and holds only
    0x1F, used as `lim - rgb.x` which promotes to int, so narrowing it is
    free. As `s32` the base emitted a VARIABLE shift (`srav`) where retail
    has a constant `sra`; `u8 lim` removes that pair from the census
    entirely. `char` and `s8` score identically -- under
    `-D__CHAR_UNSIGNED__` all three are the same value here -- so `u8` goes
    in as the honest spelling. The permuter found it as `char lim`, and the
    scorer flagged it only because the census is now a ranking key: on the
    old (length, differences) ordering this row read as 22 -> 26 and would
    have been discarded.
    REMAINING at 21: retail keeps `andi $s1,$s0,7` and then
    `andi $s0,$s1,255`, i.e. the redundant second mask survives, where gcc
    combines the two (7 & 255 == 7) into one and we emit a plain
    `addu $s0,$s1,$zero` copy. MEASURED AND DEAD, all 21: `m = h;
    m = m & 0xFF;`, `m = h; m &= 0xFF;`, `m = h;` alone, and
    `m = arg1 & 7; m = m & 0xFF;`. The one-name-across-two-statements
    fold-blocker does not reach this one. The rest is a prologue rotation --
    retail holds the parameter in `$s3` and we use `$s5`.

func_80072A48, follow-up 2026-09-04 -- a permuter output that reaches an
    EMPTY census and is still wrong, verified against the listing rather
    than assumed. output-1365-2 scores len0/cen0/100 against the base's
    len0/cen2/73 and its whole gain is moving `off += 0xC;` INSIDE the
    `if (id != 0 && func_80070920(...) == 0)` block, so the record cursor
    would only advance when the test passes.
    The listing settles it: `addiu $s5, $s5, 0xC` at 80072D08 sits in the
    DELAY SLOT of the loop-back `bnez $v0, .L80072C1C` at 80072D04, and
    `.L80072CF8` -- the label every early-out inside the body jumps to --
    is at 634F8, BEFORE that branch. A delay slot executes whether or not
    its branch is taken, so every path through the body reaches the
    increment. Retail advances unconditionally; the edit is a real
    behaviour change.
    Its four LEGITIMATE edits decompose to exactly nothing, all 222/222 and
    73: `off = func_8007058C(); k = off;`, a named `nv2 = i + 0xB; n = nv2;`,
    a named `np = e + 4;` for the store, and a `do { } while (0);` round the
    four statements the permuter had duplicated into identical arms.
    So an empty census is not a correctness check either -- it says the
    opcode MULTISET matches, and a cursor that advances on the wrong path
    executes the same opcodes.

Permuter-output sweep, 2026-09-04, the remaining four flagged rows. One win
    and three rejects, and the rejects are worth their space because each
    one names a shape to recognise.
    func_8004803C -- 119 to 86, census unchanged at four opcodes. The edit
    is legitimate and is func_8002778C's lever: DELETE the third
    `b = D_8009B45C;` reload and write the symbol inline in the three
    references that follow it. gcc hoists the address itself and puts it
    where retail does; the cached local was pinning a register. Dropping the
    reload statement while KEEPING `b` is -1 and 132, so it is the inline
    spelling that matters, not the removal.
    Remaining census: `andi -1, lhu +1, addu +1, nop -1` -- retail masks a
    value it already has in a register where we reload it.
    func_80048A28 -- REJECT. Hoists `new_var = i * 2;` above the `do` loop
    that does `i++` at its end, so the index freezes at its entry value.
    func_8005E808 -- REJECT. Deletes `w = *(s16 *)(e + 0x20);` and inlines
    the read into the test, but leaves `m = w;` standing, so `w` is read
    uninitialised. Unlike func_800528AC's case the read here IS the whole
    lever: four legitimate double-read spellings (`w = R; m = w; if (R < 0)`,
    `m = R; w = m; if (R < 0)`, `w = R; m = R; if (w < 0)`, and the same
    with the assignments swapped) are all 71, i.e. byte-identical, because
    gcc CSEs the second read into a copy. Also 71: an explicit `else` arm
    for the non-negative path. `m = R;` with no `w` at all is 77.
    Its census is the interesting part and is NOT what the permuter was
    touching: `mult +1, sll +1, lhu -1, addiu -1, addu -1, nop +1`, from a
    block where retail loads `lhu $a0,16($t0)` through a base register and
    we emit `lhu $a0,%lo(D_800F5768+16)($v0)`, with a duplicated `sll` and
    `mult` beside it. Routing those two reads through the function's
    existing `t = D_800F5768;` local is much worse (+5 and 208, with two
    extra `sw`/`lw` pairs) because `t` then lives across the calls and
    spills -- retail's base is materialised locally in that arm, so what is
    wanted is a block-scoped base, not the long-lived one.
    func_800556E8 -- NOT MEASURED, needs care. It rewrites a stored `v` as
    `*(volatile u16 *)(p + 0xE06) + sp`, which is line 43's expression; but
    `v` has three assignments (lines 43, 51, 54) and they reach different
    stores, so inlining one is only sound on one path. Check the reaching
    definitions before measuring it.

func_80045F3C -- NEW 2026-09-04, written from scratch, 214/214 with a
    TWO-opcode census (see the follow-up below; this paragraph records the
    +8 state it started from). Candidate in parked/func_80045F3C.c. NEEDS
    `as -G0` (not yet in build.py's PER_FUNC_AS_FLAGS -- add it when this
    reaches exact length). Step 0: gp=0, at=0, no jump table, so recipe
    branch 1 applies and any -G is free.
    Decode: the event-queue pop for the D_8009B45C record. Calls
    func_800773C4 on +0x15D8, ORs a bitmask from four flags at 0x15EC-0x15EF
    (each `== 3` contributing bits 20-23) and passes it to func_80076ED0,
    clears a pending-sound field under `unk40 & 0x80`, calls func_80045C98,
    and then -- if 0x7C is clear and the queue count at 0x4C is non-zero --
    dispatches on the head event's type byte, copies the 0x30-byte event
    into the header fields at 0x4E..0x78, decrements the count, and shifts
    the rest of the queue down by 0x30 with a `Msg30` struct assignment.
    `Msg30` in types.h is exactly this event: 0x30 bytes, alignment 4, with
    `kind`/`unk2`/`unk8` at the offsets used, and the listing's aligned
    `lw`/`sw` block move (no `lwl`/`lwr`) confirms the alignment.
    ADDRESSING, settled: the scalar declaration plus `as -G0` is the form.
    The default `-G8` assembler renders the bare symbol unexpanded and is
    `lui -13`; `D_8009B45C_IS_AGGREGATE` with `[0]` on every reference is
    +18 and `lw +19` because each reference becomes its own reload; the
    aggregate arm plus `-mno-split-addresses` is +45. Scalar + `as -G0` is
    `lui +2, lw +2`, i.e. two reloads too many, and nothing else has come
    close.
    THE SWITCH IS THE OPEN QUESTION and the census names it. Retail's
    dispatch is a FOUR-node comparison tree -- `beq 0x44` at the root, then
    `slti 0x45`, then `beq 0x29`, `beq 0x2A`, then `bne 0x45` -- with the
    0x44/0x45 arms SHARING one body block and 0x29/0x2A sharing another.
    Written as `case 0x44: case 0x45:` gcc's group_case_nodes merges each
    adjacent pair into a RANGE node and emits range tests instead:
    `slti +3, beq -3, bne +2, addiu -4, j -1`, five opcodes of census that
    are entirely this.
    Writing all four bodies out separately (parked as
    $SP/match/best_j3_80045F3C.c, not installed) reproduces the tree
    EXACTLY -- census drops to four opcodes, `addu -1, lui +2, lw +2,
    nop +9`, and every one of those five switch opcodes goes to zero -- at
    the cost of +12 length, because retail shares the bodies and the
    duplicate spelling does not. So the shape wanted is retail's unmerged
    nodes WITH shared bodies, and neither spelling has both yet.
    MEASURED AND DEAD: an `if`/`else if` chain with `||` (+7 but
    `sltiu +2`, gcc goes unsigned), GNU `case 0x44 ... 0x45:` ranges
    (identical to the plain shared spelling), and `goto` from separate case
    labels into a shared block (+17, and it adds two more symbol reloads).
    ALSO INSTALLED: the `unk40` read-modify-write wants the
    read-then-stores-then-writeback local (`v = *(u16 *)(base + 0x40);`
    ... two stores ... `v &= 0xFF7F;` and store back), which is +9 to +8 and
    one `nop`, and is func_8003B808's rule.
    Next: the `nop +9` has not been looked at at all.

func_80045F3C, follow-up 2026-09-04 -- +8 and nine census opcodes to
    214/214 and TWO, in one session, on three edits that the census found in
    the order it named them.
      1. THE SWITCH. Writing all four case bodies out separately reproduces
         retail's four-node comparison tree exactly and costs NOTHING in
         length -- gcc cross-jumps the duplicate bodies back together, which
         is why the earlier reading ("+12, retail shares the bodies, so this
         spelling cannot be right") was wrong. The +12 was entirely the two
         other faults below; its census summed to exactly +12 and that is
         what should have been read. With shared `case` labels
         group_case_nodes merges each adjacent pair into a RANGE node and
         emits range tests: five census opcodes, all of it this.
      2. THE `nop +9` WAS A STRUCT COPY. Retail copies +0x5C..+0x78 from
         the event at s+0x10 as a BLOCK MOVE -- four `lw` then four `sw`,
         twice -- where eight individual `*(s32 *)` assignments each carry
         their own load-delay `nop`. `Words8` in types.h is exactly eight
         words: `*(Words8 *)(D_8009B45C + 0x5C) = *(Words8 *)(s + 0x10);`
         is +12 to +5 and the nop census from +9 to +1. The neighbouring
         0x50/0x54/0x58 stores really are individual `lw`/`sw` in the
         listing, so the block starts at 0x5C and not before.
      3. TWO SYMBOL RELOADS, one per block. The `unk40` arm wants a
         block-scoped base local covering all four of its accesses (+5 to
         +3; the `v` read-writeback local is then redundant and three
         spellings score identically, so the plain compound
         `*(u16 *)(b + 0x40) = *(u16 *)(b + 0x40) & 0xFF7F;` goes in). The
         queue-shift loop wants ONE pointer local for both the byte store
         and the struct copy -- `b = D_8009B45C + off + 0x80; b[0] = s[0];
         *(Msg30 *)b = *(Msg30 *)s;` -- which is +3 to exact length.
    REMAINING, and it is a CANCELLING PAIR at exact length, so do not read
    the zero as clean: `addu -1, nop +1`, 55 differences. Retail computes
    the shift loop's destination TWICE (`addu $v1,$v1,$a1` for the `sb` off
    a base carried from the loop test, then `addu $v0,$v0,$a1` off a fresh
    reload for the copy) and puts `s += 0x30;` in the reload's load-delay
    slot where we leave a `nop`. Separating the two destination
    computations brings the `addu` back and costs a third reload per
    iteration (+3 and `lui +1, lw +1`), so the two are coupled: retail has
    two reloads per iteration and three `addu`, and no spelling has both.
    MEASURED AND DEAD, all 214/214 with the same two-opcode census:
    `s = s + 0x30;`, the bump moved inside the `if`, both orderings of it
    against `off += 0x30;` and `i++;`, and a `b = D_8009B45C + off;` base
    with `+0x80` displacements on both accesses.

# ---
func_80045F3C, follow-up 2 (2026-09-04) -- installed candidate changed from
    the 214/214 one to a +1 one, on purpose, and the reason is a sharpening
    of the ranking rule worth stating on its own.
    The 214/214 candidate's census is `addu -1, nop +1`: the deltas CANCEL,
    so its zero is two faults that happen to sum to nothing. The installed
    candidate is +1 with a census of `nop +1` alone -- the census SUMS to
    the length error, which means the length error is fully explained by one
    fault. So the test to apply at the top of the ranking is not just
    "is the length zero" but **does the census sum to the length error**.
    Sum equals the error: one fault, and you know what it is. Sum is
    smaller in absolute value than the deltas present (i.e. they cancel):
    two or more faults, and a zero there is false. This is the same trap as
    the +12 switch reading above, one level up -- there the sum EXPLAINED a
    non-zero length and the candidate was right; here the sum FAILS to
    explain a zero length and the candidate is worse.
    The edit that got there: read the base into `b` at the top of the loop
    body and write the loop's own test through `b`
    (`} while (i < *(s16 *)(b + 0x4C));`), leaving the struct copy's
    destination as an inline symbol reference. That restores retail's second
    `addu` without adding a third reload per iteration.
    REMAINING: one `nop`. Retail fills the pre-loop reload's load-delay slot
    with `s += 0x30;` -- `lui`/`lw`/`addiu $s0,$s0,48`/`lh $v0,76($v1)` --
    and gcc sinks ours into the loop preheader instead, leaving the slot
    empty. Retail's byte store then reuses that same reload's register
    (`addu $v1,$v1,$a1`), so the guard read and the body's `sb` share one
    symbol load.
    MEASURED AND DEAD for the bump, ALL of them +1 with the identical
    one-opcode census, which is the wrong-axis tell: `do { s += 0x30; }
    while (0);`, `s = &s[0x30];`, `s = s + 0x30;`, the bump moved above the
    `func_80045514()` call, moved above the 0x4C decrement, moved inside the
    `if`, and a named `n = *(s16 *)(D_8009B45C + 0x4C);` guard local with
    the bump both before and after it.
    ALSO DEAD: assigning the base at the END of the loop body so it carries
    across the back edge (+2, `addu +1`), with or without the bump moved;
    and hoisting the base above the guard so the guard tests `b` too (-1,
    one reload short).

func_800482B0 -- NEW 2026-09-04, written from scratch, +2 of 234 on the
    first afternoon. Candidate in parked/func_800482B0.c. NEEDS `as -G0`
    (same as func_80045F3C; not yet in PER_FUNC_AS_FLAGS). Step 0: gp=0,
    at=0, no jump table, no block move, no runtime divide, and D_8009B45C
    is the ONLY symbol it touches -- so recipe branch 1 and the scalar
    declaration, exactly as for func_80045F3C.
    Decode: the sound-channel allocator. Returns early if the id's entry in
    the table at *(s32 *)(D_8009B45C + 0x43C) is 0xFFFF, and again through
    func_800451E0 if bit 15 of the id is set. Then it picks a channel four
    ways in turn: an explicit mask in the low nibble of arg4 (first set bit,
    via func_80047F38), an occurrence count in the high nibble (scan the
    four channels for the Nth already playing this id), a round-robin from
    the cursor at 0x435 looking for a silent channel, and finally a priority
    comparison against the per-channel byte table at 0x40C. Each of the
    four ends in the same seven-argument func_8004803C call.
    TWO DECODE ERRORS FOUND AND FIXED, both by reading the listing rather
    than m2c: the table at 0x40C is stride ONE (`addu $v0,$v0,$a1` with the
    raw channel byte, then `lbu 0x40C($v0)`), where m2c's struct-array
    notation implied a wider stride; and the 0x404 table is stride TWO with
    `lhu`. The seven-argument call site count checks out -- three
    `sw ...,0x10($sp)` against three `jal func_8004803C`, so the three sites
    are genuinely separate and none was cross-jumped.
    THE OPEN PROBLEM IS THE FRAME, and it is worth stating precisely
    because it makes every stack reference in the diff wrong at once.
    Retail's frame is 88 bytes: three stack locals at 0x20 (s16, the
    out-parameter of func_80077C50), 0x22 (u8) and 0x28 (u16), with the
    callee-saved block starting at 0x30. Ours is 104: `arg1` is SPILLED to
    the stack (`sh $a1,40($sp)`, an instruction retail does not have) and
    the three locals land at 0x28, 0x30 and 0x38 with the saved block
    pushed to 0x40. Retail instead keeps arg1 in `$fp` with an
    `addu $fp,$a1,$zero` as the third instruction of the prologue, which is
    WORKFLOW's parameter-copy-in-the-prologue shape. So the fix is register
    pressure: the draft declares seven `s32` locals (m, i, n, bit, done, c,
    hi) where retail gets by on s0-s7 plus fp, and the gap between 0x22 and
    0x28 says retail has a fourth stack object I have not identified.
    Census at +2: `addiu +2, beq +2, j +1, sh +1, lhu +3, andi -1, bne -2,
    lbu -1, nop -1, sll -1, srl -1`, which sums to +2. The `srl -1` is
    retail's `srl $s5,$v0,4` -- the high nibble computed ONCE into a
    register -- and a named `hi = (arg4 & 0xF0) >> 4;` local does NOT
    produce it (byte-identical to the inline spelling), so that wants an
    unsigned intermediate or a different expression; try it again after the
    frame is right, since everything here is being measured through a
    displaced stack.

func_800482B0, follow-up 2026-09-04 -- +2 to -1 and THE FRAME IS NOW EXACT,
    on one edit: merging two `s32` locals that never coexist.
    The frame was 104 against retail's 88 with `arg1` spilled to the stack,
    and the diagnosis in the entry above ("register pressure, seven locals
    where retail uses s0-s7 plus fp") was right. Merging `done` into `n` --
    `n` is live only in the occurrence-count scan and `done` only in the two
    round-robin loops, so they never overlap -- drops the spill entirely.
    The stack is then 0x20 (s16, the func_80077C50 out-parameter), 0x22 (u8)
    and 0x28 (u16) with the saved block at 0x30, which is retail's layout
    exactly, and the frame is 88.
    THE 0x22-TO-0x28 GAP IS NOT A FOURTH OBJECT. The entry above guessed it
    was; it is not. With the spill gone the three locals pack at 2, 1 and
    then 0x28, and the hole is just padding before the word-aligned saved
    block. Trying to fill it with `u8 sp22[6]` is WORSE and instructive:
    gcc allocates arrays before scalars, so the array took 0x20 and pushed
    the s16 out to 0x28 -- the layout got further from retail, not closer.
    Merging `bit` into `m` as well (they also never coexist) is the same -1
    but `addu +2` instead of `+1` and one more `nop` gone, so it is a
    lateral move; the single merge is what is installed.
    THE HIGH-NIBBLE SPELLING, re-measured now the stack is not displaced --
    and the earlier "byte-identical, so this axis is closed" reading was an
    artefact of measuring through the bad frame. `hi = (arg4 & 0xF0) >> 4;`
    and `(arg4 >> 4) & 0xF` both still leave `srl -1`, but
    `u32 hi = (u32)(arg4 & 0xF0) >> 4;` DOES produce retail's
    `srl $s5,$v0,4` -- the srl entry disappears from the census -- at the
    cost of one `sra`. Same ten opcodes either way, so neither is installed
    yet; the unsigned intermediate is the structurally right one and should
    go in once the sra is understood.
    REMAINING at -1, census `addiu +2, addu +1, beq +2, j +1, andi -1,
    bne -2, lbu -1, nop -1, sll -1, srl -1`, summing to -1. The diff count
    (232) is meaningless: the FIRST row differs because cc1psx hoists the
    `lui`/`lw` of D_8009B45C above the frame setup where retail emits it
    fourth, and everything after is shifted. Retail's prologue is
    `addiu $sp` / `sw $fp` / `addu $fp,$a1,$zero` / `lw $a1,0x68($sp)` --
    arg1 into `$fp` and arg4 reloaded -- where we put arg4 in `$s7` and
    arg0 in `$s6` and never use `$fp` for a parameter. That is allocation
    and scheduling, i.e. the permuter's class, and the function is now
    structurally right enough to hand to it.

func_800482B0, follow-up 2 (2026-09-04) -- two negatives and a tooling fix.
    `as -G0` is now in build.py's PER_FUNC_AS_FLAGS for this function and for
    func_80045F3C. Both are inert for the build (neither is in src/), but
    permute.py reads PER_FUNC_AS_FLAGS directly, so WITHOUT the entry the
    permuter would have been measuring these against the wrong assembler
    flags for its entire run. That is worth knowing before handing any
    -G0 function to it.
    CLOSED AXIS: retail's prologue copies arg0 TWICE --
    `addu $t0,$a0,$zero` then `addu $s4,$t0,$zero` -- and uses `$t0` for the
    `& 0x8000` test and `$s4` for every `& 0xFFFF` reference, which reads
    exactly like func_8003BF00's two-names-for-one-parameter shape. It is
    not reachable that way here: a second local `p = arg0;` used for the
    test, used for all the masked references, or used for the masked
    references with the test on `arg0`, are all THREE byte-identical to the
    single-name version. gcc coalesces the copy, and the derived-offset
    trick that normally forces one has nowhere to go on a parameter that is
    only ever masked.
    Handed to the permuter, which is the right tool for what is left:
    census `addiu +2, addu +1, beq +2, j +1, andi -1, bne -2, lbu -1,
    nop -1, sll -1, srl -1` at -1, with the whole diff shifted because
    cc1psx hoists the D_8009B45C load above the frame setup. The `beq +2 /
    bne -2` pair is two tests whose polarity is inverted somewhere and is
    the most promising hand lever left, but it cannot be located by a
    positional diff while the prologue is shifted -- find it by re-syncing
    the two streams on their matching blocks rather than by row index.

func_800482B0, follow-up 3 (2026-09-04) -- the BLOCK LAYOUT was wrong, and
    finding it needed a diagnostic the project did not have.
    Reading the target's LABEL ORDER rather than its instructions settles
    it: after the entry test and the `arg0 & 0x8000` arm's
    `func_800451E0` + return, retail emits `.L8004833C` / `.L80048358` /
    `.L80048364` -- the two channel-store arms and the SHARED
    `func_8004803C` call -- and only THEN `.L8004839C`, the mask scan that
    runs first. So the call block is a `goto` target written INSIDE the
    returning branch, which is func_8002538C's rule. Moving `play:` and its
    call up between `return;` and the closing brace of the
    `if ((arg0 & 0x8000) != 0)` block removes two whole census entries
    (`addu +1` and `j +1` vanish; ten opcodes to eight).
    The two arms keep their own copies of the `sra` for `(s16)arg1` and the
    `lbu` of sp22 and share only from `.L80048364`, which is the
    cross-jump stopping where the arms first differ -- arm A needs a symbol
    reload and arm B already has the base in `$v1`. And the second argument
    is `lbu $a1,0x435($v1)`, i.e. the channel is READ BACK from
    D_8009B45C after being stored, which the candidate already does.
    THE MEASUREMENT THAT FOUND IT is worth more than the edit. Once the
    FIRST row differs -- here because cc1psx hoists the D_8009B45C load
    above the frame setup -- the difference count is worthless and so is
    any fault located by row index. Aligning the two OPCODE streams
    (difflib matching blocks over just the mnemonics) is position
    independent and still measures structure: this edit took it from
    173/235 to 205/234 while the difference count said nothing. It is now
    printed by $SP/diag.py on every run.
    STATE: -3 of 234, census `addiu +2, beq +2, andi -1, bne -2, lbu -1,
    nop -1, sll -1, srl -1`, summing to -3. The `beq +2 / bne -2` pair is
    still the best hand lever and is now locatable, since the streams align

    previous one was -1: it is installed anyway because both censuses sum

func_800482B0, follow-up 4 (2026-09-04) -- -3 to -1, census eight opcodes to
    FOUR, opcode-stream alignment 205/234 to 219/234. Two edits, both found
    by walking the gaps between the aligned streams rather than by row
    index.
      1. THE MASK-SCAN LOOP TESTS THE WRONG WAY. Written as
         `while ((m & 1) == 0) { c++; m >>= 1; if ((u8)c >= 4) goto scan; }`
         the built code carries `addiu $s0,$s0,1` immediately followed by
         `addiu $s0,$s0,-1` -- gcc rotating the loop and then compensating
         the counter on the way out. Retail tests to EXIT
         (`bnez $v0,.L8004833C` at the top of the body) and its counter is
         never compensated. Writing it as
         `do { if ((m & 1) != 0) goto found_a; c++; m >>= 1; }
         while ((u8)c < 4); goto scan;` removes both the `beq +2` and the
         `addiu +2` census entries at once.
         The two `D_8009B45C[0x435] = c;` stores also move up into the
         early block as labelled entries `found_a:` and `found_b:` ahead of
         `play:`, which is retail's `.L8004833C` / `.L80048358` /
         `.L80048364` exactly -- two arms that each store the channel and
         share only the call.
      2. THERE IS A FLAG TEST BEFORE THE ROUND-ROBIN BLOCK that the draft
         did not have. Retail has `andi $v0,$s3,255` / `bne $v0,$zero,<end>`
         between the occurrence scan and the round-robin loop, i.e. an
         explicit `if ((u8)done != 0) return;`. m2c had rendered it as
         `if (!(0U & 0xFF))` -- it had PROVED the flag zero there and folded
         the test away, which is exactly why reading m2c's output rather
         than the listing hid it. Adding the guard is -6 to -1 and takes the
         alignment from 210 to 219.
    MEASURED AND DEAD: hoisting `(s32)(s16)sp28` into a local before the
    round-robin block, at either side of the new guard (-4, five opcodes,
    alignment 204 and 206) -- retail recomputes the conversion at each of
    the three call sites and does not share it. Also still lateral: the
    `u32 hi = (u32)(arg4 & 0xF0) >> 4;` spelling, which trades `srl -1` for
    `sra -1` at identical alignment.
    REMAINING at -1: `lbu -1, nop +2, sll -1, srl -1`, summing to -1.

func_800482B0, follow-up 5 (2026-09-04) -- the remaining four census opcodes
    are ONE fault, and it is a loop-invariant hoist.
    Walking the gaps: retail has `andi $a0,$s4,0xFFFF` at T[70] and
    `andi $v0,$s4,0xFFFF` at T[94] and `sll $a2,$fp,16` at T[100], and we
    have a `nop` at all three. We instead emit `andi $s4,$s6,0xFFFF` ONCE,
    in the occurrence loop's preheader. So gcc hoists `arg0 & 0xFFFF` out
    of the loop as an invariant and then has nothing left to fill three
    load-delay slots with; retail recomputes the mask at each use and the
    recomputations ARE the slot fillers. That single fact accounts for
    `andi -1`, `sll -1` and `nop +2` together, and probably `lbu -1` too.
    MEASURED AND DEAD, all identical at -1 with the same four-opcode census
    and alignment 219/234: flipping the comparison so the masked value is
    on the left, writing the mask as `(u16)arg0`, and both together. The
    comparison-operand axis is closed here.
    What is wanted is a spelling gcc will not hoist. Not yet found.
    ALSO: THE PERMUTER HAD BEEN RUNNING FROM A STALE BASE for 5300
    iterations. It was started before the loop-form and guard edits, and
    its best stored output re-scores at ten census opcodes and alignment
    193/234 against the installed candidate's four and 219 -- i.e. every
    one of those iterations was mutating a candidate two levers behind.
    WORKFLOW already says to rerun it from an improved base; the concrete
    cost of not doing so is now measured. Restart the permuter every time
    the installed candidate improves.

func_800482B0, follow-up 6 (2026-09-04) -- -1 to 234/234 with an EMPTY
    census and alignment 224/234. Two edits.
      1. THE CALL IS WRITTEN IN BOTH ARMS, not once behind a shared label.
         Retail's `andi $a0,$s4,0xFFFF` and `sll $a2,$fp,16` sit in the
         DELAY SLOTS of the two branches into the play block -- that is
         duplicated argument setup, func_80030998's shape, and it is why
         the census carried `nop +2` beside `andi -1` and `sll -1`. The
         earlier diagnosis in follow-up 5 ("gcc hoists arg0 & 0xFFFF out of
         the loop as an invariant") was WRONG: the hoist is real but it is
         a consequence, not the fault. With `found_a:` and `found_b:` each
         carrying their own copy of the call and return, gcc cross-jumps
         the shared tail and fills both slots. -1 and four opcodes to
         234/234 and two.
         Keeping the shared `play:` label and merely adding a second
         `goto play;` is unchanged, so it is the duplication that matters,
         not the branch structure.
      2. The two remaining opcodes were the `srl`/`sra` swap, and on this
         base `u32 hi = (u32)(arg4 & 0xF0) >> 4;` finally closes it --
         census EMPTY. It had been measured twice before and called
         "lateral" both times, on bases that were -6 and -1; this is the
         third instance today of a spelling that only becomes right once
         the faults around it are gone.

func_80023144, follow-up 2026-09-04 -- +6 to +10, and that is an IMPROVEMENT.
    I had rejected the volatile guard an hour ago on the length key alone.
    That was the wrong key and the entry above says so incorrectly. The +6
    candidate's census was `addiu +2, addu +3, andi +2, lbu +2, lui -2,
    sb -1`: the two negatives are the store/reload/store sequence retail has
    and we lacked, i.e. FIVE missing instructions cancelling against eleven
    extra ones. `D_8009B344_SIZED8_VOLATILE` supplies exactly those five --
    the six-instruction `sb %lo` / `lui` / `lbu %lo` / `nop` / `addiu 0x17`
    / `sb %lo` block matches retail instruction for instruction -- and the
    census becomes all-positive with one fewer distinct opcode. By the
    ranking as written (sum, then distinct opcodes) the volatile candidate
    wins; I ranked on |length| and got it backwards.
    THEN the named flag read. Retail issues `lhu $v0,0x16(rec)` BEFORE the
    stores to D_8009B34E and D_8009B355 in two separate blocks, so the store
    covers the load's delay slot; we store first and take a `nop`. Writing
    `fl = *(u16 *)(e + 0x16);` above the store group is func_800434F4's
    lever and closes the `nop` entry: alignment 198/210, four census
    opcodes.
    ONLY ONE OF THE TWO SITES WANTS IT. Applying the read at the first site
    and at the second site score IDENTICALLY (+10, four opcodes, 198), and
    applying it at BOTH is one instruction short (`nop -1`, +9, five
    opcodes). The first site is installed; the second is equivalent and
    untried in combination with anything else.
    REMAINING: `addiu +2, addu +3, andi +2, lbu +3`, summing to +10, all
    positive -- ten instructions we emit and retail does not. The `lbu +3`
    and `andi +2` are the place to start.

func_80023144, follow-up 2 -- +10 to +3, census four opcodes to three,
    alignment 198/210 to 202/210. Two edits and one bug caught.
      1. THE `mode` COPY WAS WRITTEN THREE TIMES. `v = mode;` before the
         `unk17 == 3` block, again inside it, and `v = mode + 2;` in the
         `== 2` block -- m2c has only two, and the census's `addu +3` was
         exactly those three `addu $a1,$s3,$zero` copies. ONE assignment,
         placed after the `== 3` block's closing brace so it picks up the
         `mode += 4`, removes the `addu` entry outright.
         FIRST ATTEMPT AT THIS WAS A BUG and the score hid it: a scripted
         edit dropped the copy in at the wrong brace, INSIDE the `== 3`
         block, so `v` was uninitialised whenever that branch did not run.
         It compiled and scored +9 with four opcodes -- better than the
         correct +10 -- which is the uninitialised-read reject shape
         arriving from my own edit rather than from the permuter. Placing
         it correctly is +7 and three opcodes, so the right version wins
         anyway; it would not always.
      2. `arg0[0x17]` WAS READ THREE TIMES, retail reads it twice. The
         `== 3` test and the `D_801D5608[2] = arg0[0x17];` store are in
         different blocks so gcc cannot common them. Naming it --
         `k = arg0[0x17];` with the test and the store both on `k` -- is
         +7 to +3 and takes `addiu +2` with it. Using the same name for the
         later `== 2` test as well is worse (four opcodes, alignment 201),
         so retail really does re-read for that one.
    REMAINING: `andi +2, lbu +2, nop -1`, summing to +3.

func_80023144, follow-up 3 -- +3 to -1, census three opcodes to ONE, 73
    differences, alignment 202/210. One declaration.
    `c` held `D_800E9FF0[side].unk19`, which is an `s8` field, and I had
    declared the local `s8` to match. That is the narrow-local trap: retail
    reads the field ONCE with `lb` and then does `sltiu $v0,$v0,4` on the
    same sign-extended register, while the `s8` local made gcc read it a
    SECOND time with `lbu` and mask with `andi 0xFF` to get `(u32)c`. Two
    sites, so `lbu +2, andi +2`, which is the whole of the remaining +3
    (with `addiu +2` already closed). `s32 c` -- the widest natural type,
    cast at the point of use -- removes all four. `(u8)c >= 4` on the `s8`
    local instead is +3 and unchanged, so it is the DECLARATION and not the
    comparison.
    REMAINING: one `nop`. In two places we fill a load-delay slot that
    retail leaves empty -- the `lbu %lo(D_8009B344)` reload (we put the
    `addu $a0,$s0,$zero` call argument there, retail puts it in the `jal`'s
    own slot) and the `lw 0(arg0)` before the final `lbu 0x16` (we put the
    call-result copy there). Both are the scheduler having an instruction
    available that retail's did not. Not yet closed.

func_80023144, follow-up 4 -- -1 to 210/210, 73 differences to 37,
    alignment 205/210. Two edits and a refinement to the ranking rule.
      1. A SEPARATE NAME FOR THE CALL RESULT. `o` held both the object
         pointer read out of `arg0` and the result of func_80035BE4, which
         costs an extra `addu`; `p` for the pointer and `o` for the result
         is -1 with the census going from `nop -1` to `addu -1` and the
         alignment 202 to 205.
      2. THE `v` VARIABLE DOES NOT EXIST. Dropping it entirely -- `mode`
         carries the value, `mode = mode + 2;` in the `== 2` block, and
         `mode` passed to the call -- is exact length and HALVES the
         difference count, 73 to 37, at the same alignment. This is the
         third measurement of that shape today: it was worse at +6 and at
         +10 and is right here, which is the re-measure rule paying out
         again.
    THE RANKING REFINEMENT, and it matters. The installed candidate's census
    is `addu -1, nop +1` -- two entries that CANCEL, which my own rule calls
    a false zero. Reading the gap shows it is ONE fault: at T155 retail has
    `addu $a1,$s2,$zero` and we have a `nop` in the same slot. An
    instruction REPLACED BY A NOP shows up as a cancelling pair and is a
    single substitution, not two independent faults. So "the deltas cancel"
    is not by itself proof of two faults -- walk the gap and see whether the
    two entries land in the same slot before believing it.
    REMAINING at 210/210: that one `addu $a1,$s2,$zero`; the
    `addu $a0,$s0,$zero` call argument, which retail puts in the `jal`'s
    delay slot while we use it to fill the preceding `lbu`'s; and
    `%hi(D_800E9FF0)` against `%hi(D_801D5608)` materialised in the other
    order at two sites.

func_80023144, follow-up 5 -- hand plateau at 210/210 and 37 differences.
    The missing `addu` is a DELAY-SLOT DUPLICATION, not a missing copy.
    Retail has `addu $a1,$s2,$zero` TWICE: once after the `mode += 2` in the
    `arg0[0x17] == 2` block (we have that one, and the whole surrounding
    block matches instruction for instruction) and once in the delay slot of
    the `bne` that skips the block -- gcc redirecting the branch to
    target+4 and duplicating the join's first instruction. We emit a `nop`
    there instead. That is a scheduler decision with no obvious source
    handle.

    nested `if`s, `mode += 2;` for `mode = mode + 2;`, and (worse, +2)

    D_800E9FF0 index computation, and splits its `%lo` across the `lb`; we
    emit the pair together and later. Both symbols are unsized arrays so
    cc1psx's splittable pair is available to us -- this is scheduling, not
    addressing.

func_80031874 -- NEW 2026-09-04, written from scratch, -1 of 280, census 17
    opcodes, alignment 232/280. Candidate in parked/func_80031874.c. Step 0:
    gp=4, at=0, no jump table, no runtime divide -- default flags, no
    addressing work at all.
    Decode: it draws a duel-side status panel. Sets up two hardware primitive
    buffers at 0x1F800020 and 0x1F800060, writes a colour and size to each,
    positions them from `arg0`'s +0x30/+0x32 minus D_8009B146/D_8009B148,
    picks a palette row out of D_80090DD8 by the side byte at `arg0[0x67]`,
    then loops eight times over 0x10-byte card slots emitting numbers through
    func_800358A0/func_800357E8 and func_800316F0.
    M2C GOT A THIRD STRIDE WRONG, and it is worth stating as a pattern now:
    it rendered `D_8009B2FC + s6 * 4 + 4` as `D_8009B2FC + (s6 * 0x2D4C + 4)`
    -- inventing a 0x2D4C structure from what the listing plainly shows as
    `sll $v0,$v0,2`. It also rendered the `D_8009B2FC + s2 + 0x606A` byte
    reads as a struct index when `addu $v0,$v0,$s2` is stride ONE. Read the
    shift and the addu before believing any m2c stride.
    THE HARDWARE ADDRESSES ARE BASE LOCALS, INCLUDING IN CALL ARGUMENTS.
    WORKFLOW's func_8002A9C0 entry says to use a base local per block but to
    keep the call arguments as literals; here that is wrong, and the census
    says so loudly. With literals the first draft carried `lui +18, ori +8,
    addu -27` -- retail materialises each of 0x1F800020, 0x1F800060 and
    0x1F800000 ONCE and copies the register at every call, where literals
    give a fresh lui/ori pair per call site. Routing the call arguments
    through the locals too is `lui +18` to `-3` and the alignment 213 to 232.
    So the "keep call arguments as literals" advice is specific to
    func_8002A9C0's shape, not general.
    REMAINING at -1: `addu -5, nop +4, andi +3, sra +2, sll -2, sh -2` and
    eleven smaller entries. The `andi +3` and `sra +2` are width and
    signedness on the byte fields -- the same class that was worth four
    opcodes on func_80023144 -- and are the place to start.

func_80031874, RETRACTION 2026-09-04. The entry two above says "m2c got a
    third stride wrong" and that it "rendered `D_8009B2FC + s6 * 4 + 4` as
    `D_8009B2FC + (s6 * 0x2D4C + 4)`, inventing a 0x2D4C structure from what
    the listing plainly shows as `sll $v0,$v0,2`". THAT IS WRONG. m2c was
    right and I misread the listing.
    The scaling is a MULTIPLY-BY-CONSTANT EXPANSION, nine instructions long,
    and I read only its last step: `sll $s6,3` / `addu $s6` (x9) /
    `sll 4` (x144) / `addu $s6` (x145) / `sll 2` + `addu` (x725) /
    `sll 2` (x2900) / `subu $s6` (x2899) / `sll 2` = **0x2D4C**. The
    `sll $v0,$v0,2` I quoted is the final doubling of that chain, not the
    whole index. Writing `s6 * 0x2D4C + 4` is what closes the `sll -4`
    entry: census 14 opcodes to 12, alignment 236/280 to 249/280,
    differences 252 to 242.
    THE LESSON IS SPECIFIC AND WORTH KEEPING. A shift immediately before the
    `addu` that forms an address is NOT necessarily the whole scale factor.
    When m2c reports a large odd stride, look for a run of
    `sll`/`addu`/`subu` on ONE register and evaluate the whole chain before
    contradicting it -- a large constant that is not a power of two is
    expanded, not multiplied, and the expansion looks exactly like ordinary
    index arithmetic if you read only its tail. The other two m2c stride
    corrections today (func_800482B0's 0x40C table and this function's
    stride-one `+0x606A` reads) were checked against `addu $v0,$v0,$s2`
    with no shift at all and stand.
    REMAINING at +1: `nop +6, sh -4, andi +3, lui -3, addu -2, sw +2` and
    five smaller entries.

func_80031874, follow-up 6 -- census 11 opcodes to 10, alignment 251/280 to
    253/280, and the `sw +2` entry gone. Two stores of small constants into
    the hardware buffer's `+8` field are HALFWORD, not word:
    `*(u16 *)(g + 8) = 0x18;` and `= 8;` where I had written `*(s32 *)`.
    HOW IT WAS FOUND is the part worth keeping. The census said `sh -4,
    sw +2` and I had been reading that as two separate faults. Counting the
    stores by OFFSET rather than by opcode -- a Counter over the `sh` lines
    with the register names normalised away -- shows retail has TWO
    `sh R,8(R)` and we have NONE, while every other offset matches or is
    off by one. That is one fault, not two: the same two stores appear in
    our column as `sw`. So when a census carries a store opcode in one
    direction and another in the other, bucket the stores by their
    displacement before looking for a source lever; the offsets name the
    site immediately where the totals cannot.
    The neighbouring `*(s32 *)(g + 8) = 0x80008;` and `*(s32 *)(h + 8) =
    0x100010;` really are words -- retail has `sw $a2,0x8($s0)` and
    `sw $v1,0x8($t2)` for those -- so the field is written both ways and
    only the small constants are halfwords.
    MEASURED AND DEAD: declaring the hardware pointers volatile, in three
    scopes (the `g` halfword accesses alone, plus the `g` words, plus the
    `h` accesses). None touches `sh -4` and all add nops -- +3 to +5 length
    and the alignment down to 239-243. The hardware registers are not
    volatile in the original.
    REMAINING at -2, 253/280, 251 differences: `sh -2` (one store each at
    the `+4` and `+6` offsets, retail 11 and 9 against our 10 and 8),
    `nop +3, lui -3, ori -2` and six smaller.

func_80031874, follow-up 7 -- -2 to 280/280, differences 251 to 239, on
    STORE ORDER inside two arms of one `if`. The `s6 == 0` branch writes
    `h + 4` and `h + 6` in both arms, and the `h + 6` store is textually
    identical in each; gcc cross-jumps it and emits one copy where retail
    has two. Writing the `+6` store BEFORE the `+4` store in both arms
    recovers it -- the arms then differ at the instruction before the join,
    which is where find_cross_jump gives up.
    Two other shapes for the same merge are worth recording as dead, both
    -2 and 251 differences, i.e. no change: assigning `pal` first in each
    arm, and rewriting the whole thing as an early `goto` past the else. It
    is specifically the ORDER of the two stores, not the arm structure.
    The offset buckets confirm it cleanly: the `sh R,6(R)` count went 8
    against retail's 9 to 9 against 9, and the only store bucket still
    differing is `sh R,4(R)` at 10 against 11.
    REMAINING at 280/280, 253/280, 239 differences: `sh -1` (one store at
    the `+4` offset), `nop +3, lui -3, addiu +2, ori -2` and five smaller.

func_80023144, follow-up 6 -- 210/210 with an EMPTY census, alignment
    205/210 to 206/210, 37 differences to 36. INSTALLED FROM THE PERMUTER
    AND THE SHAPE IS NOT PLAUSIBLE SOURCE -- flagged here so nobody reads it
    as a finding about the original.
    Its edit wraps `D_801D5608[2] = 3;` in an `if`/`else` whose two arms are
    IDENTICAL, with the condition reading
    `D_800E9FF0[D_8009B1D5 ^ 1].unk19`. It is legal -- both arms do the same
    thing so the condition's value cannot matter, and the field is
    initialised, so this is not the uninitialised-read reject shape. It is
    simply not something anyone writes.
    WHAT IT IS NOT is a block boundary. That was the obvious reading, and
    the two substitutes that test it both come back at the BASE score
    (37, two census opcodes, 205): `do { D_801D5608[2] = 3; } while (0);`
    and the same identical-arm `if` with the condition on `c`, a value
    already in hand. So the lever is specifically the EXTRA READ of that
    field, not the shape around it -- which is the opposite of
    func_800528AC, where the identical-arm `if` WAS just a block boundary
    and `do { } while (0);` reached it.
    THE PLAUSIBLE SPELLING WAS LOOKED FOR AND NOT FOUND. The natural way to
    make that field be read twice is to write the side choice as a default
    plus a correction rather than as an index -- `c = D_800E9FF0[B1D5 ^ 1]
    .unk19; if (arg0[0x10] >= 2) c = D_800E9FF0[B1D5].unk19;` and its
    mirror. Both are semantically identical to the installed `side` index
    and both are much worse: +5 and 199/210, +2 and 200/210.
    Kept as the park because a park is working material and the improvement
    is real; it must NOT go to src/ in this shape.
